# MileWage build progress log

Running log for the Gate 4 build (roadmap `17`). Updated and pushed after each PR.

## Environment

- Host `coder` has Node 24.15.0 as default. Node 22.22.0 was extracted from the local Docker image `node:22.22.0-bookworm-slim` into `~/.local/node22` and put first on `PATH` for all work. `.nvmrc` is `22.22.0`. All `verify` runs below used Node 22.22.0 / npm 10.9.4.
- Merge method: `git merge --squash` of the PR branch into `main` (roadmap 17 sec. 1 rule 3), run locally because no GitLab host or branch protection exists yet. Branches are pushed to origin as well.

## PR 1 — [M0.1] Repo skeleton, workspaces, tooling, verify

- Branch `pr-01-repo-skeleton`; squash commit on main `43d2d20`.
- Done: npm workspaces (`apps/*`, `packages/*`), `.nvmrc`, `tsconfig.base.json` (strict, ESM), ESLint flat config + Prettier, Vitest root config, `.gitignore`, README, placeholder package + test in `shared`, `track-engine`, `server`. `npm run verify` = typecheck + lint (eslint + prettier check) + test.
- Lint rule: `track-engine/src` forbids `react-native`, `node:*`, `fs`, `path`, DB and network libs, `Date.now`, `Math.random`, `fetch`. Proven by `packages/track-engine/test/lint-rule.test.ts` (bad samples fail, same code passes outside track-engine).
- Tests: 12 passed. Size: about 297 changed lines excluding lockfile.
- Deviation: roadmap says this PR makes the repo's initial commit including `plans/`; the repo already had commits (plans committed earlier), so this is an ordinary commit.

## PR 3 — [M0.3] Module registry, dependency graph, event bus

- Branch `pr-03-module-registry`; squash commit on main `2577247`.
- Done in `packages/shared`: `defineModule`, `buildRegistry` (cycle, unknown/duplicate module, non-toggleable-off and missing-dependency refusal with plain messages; dependency-ordered start), `resolveModuleConfig` (default, `config/settings.json` `modules`, then `MILEWAGE_MODULE_<NAME>=0|1`), typed `createEventBus` with the nine event names of 08 section 16.4, `moduleMatrix` test helper (all-on, only-X-with-dependencies, missing-dependency).

- Not done (per PR text): the `stops` runtime guard, real modules, hot toggling, plugin loader.

## PR 4 — [M0.4] OpenAPI as source of truth

- Branch `pr-04-openapi`; squash commit on main `b87da35`.
- Done: 08 section 13 copied verbatim to `packages/shared/openapi/openapi.yaml` (excluded from Prettier to keep it a verbatim copy); `scripts/openapi-generate.mjs` (`generate`, `check`, `lint`) writes `packages/shared/generated/api-types.ts` and `schemas.json`; `npm run openapi:check` (drift check + `x-modules` lint) is part of `verify`.
- Tool choice (made in this PR as required): `openapi-typescript` v7 for types (maintained, OpenAPI 3.1, pure types, no runtime). JSON Schemas need no tool: 3.1 schemas are already JSON Schema 2020-12, so the script moves `components.schemas` to `$defs` and rewrites the refs. Parsing uses `yaml`.
- Tests: 39 passed in total (7 new): committed files match fresh generation; editing a generated file fails; editing the spec without regenerating fails; the real spec maps all 61 operations to exactly one module; unmapped / doubly mapped / unknown operations fail the lint.
- Size: about 185 changed lines excluding the verbatim spec copy, generated files and lockfile (the YAML copy is itself ~1,965 lines; treated as a verbatim copy of 08 section 13, not authored code).
- Deviation/open point: the `stops` module is mapped in `x-modules` as written in 08; `putTripOverride` sits in `tracking` there although 08 section 16.2's table lists it under none; kept as the spec's YAML says.

## PR 6 — [M1.2] Stage 3 gate, distance, live accumulator

- Branch `pr-06-gate-distance-accumulator`; squash commit on main `cd9abad`.
- Done: written-out haversine (R = 6,371,000 m, rounded once to decimetres), `gateStep`/`gate` per 14 section 5.3 (non-positive dt, speed over 420 dm/s, under 200 dm, anchor moves only on accept, integer comparisons), `measureDistance` (stages 1-3 batch), `createLiveAccumulator(startedAt)` running stages 1-3 one fix at a time with the same functions.
- Tests: 63 passed in total (13 new): straight line, closed square loop, single spike excluded, 20 m gate (19.9 dropped, 20 accepted, creep accumulates), 42 m/s boundary, non-positive dt, empty/single fix, low-accuracy fix never anchors, accumulator equals batch on clean and on messy in-order input, no `Date.now` calls.
- Decisions: the live accumulator checks only the lower window bound (shift end unknown) and expects fixes in time order; an out-of-order live fix is dropped as non-positive dt, which is why batch (which sorts) is the authority. `processShift` is not built here (later PRs).
- Size: about 275 changed lines.

## PR 7 — [M1.3] Seeded ride generator, golden fixtures, version-bump guard

- Branch `pr-07-ride-generator-golden`; squash commit on main `5e2c6b8`.
- Done: test-support generator `test/support/rides.ts` (mulberry32 seeds; straight, grid with turns, closed loop, stop-and-go with 3-minute jittered waits; each with an analytic truth length), `test/fixtures/golden.json` (expected outputs per ride plus an append-only ledger of one output digest per `algorithmVersion`), guard in `test/support/golden.ts`. Regenerate with `UPDATE_FIXTURES=1 npx vitest run golden`; it refuses to rewrite the entry of an existing version.
- Guard design: a digest ledger instead of comparing with a previous commit, so regenerating fixtures without bumping `algorithmVersion` is refused (a simple "fixtures match code" test would pass after a silent regeneration). Reviewers must still check that the ledger only ever gains lines (no CI diff check yet).
- Tests: 80 passed in total (17 new): determinism, seeds change noise not truth, engine totals within 2 m + 0.2% of analytic truth, jitter in waits adds nothing, guard fails on a simulated output change and on a simulated threshold change, bumped version demands regeneration, regeneration adds a ledger entry.
- Deviation: expected JSON is the stage 1-3 part of `ShiftResult` (version, `totals.totalM`, stats counters) because `processShift` does not exist yet; later PRs extend it. Size: about 315 changed lines excluding `golden.json`.

- Branch `pr-09-db-migrations`; squash commit on main `99d5701`.
- Done in `apps/server/src/db`: `openDatabase` (better-sqlite3 single write connection; PRAGMAs of 10 section 3.4 applied and read back on every open, failing if SQLite ignores one), forward-only `migrate` (`schema_migrations(module, id)`, one transaction for all pending so a failure rolls everything back, lock file `<db>.migrate.lock`, `beforeMigrate(pending)` no-op hook for the M3.4 pre-migration backup, refusal of a database that records an unknown migration), per-module SQL folders (`migrations/core/0001_identity.sql`: `users`, `settings`, `instance` exactly as in 07 section 2.1), Drizzle schema, `createUserRepository` (count, findByUsername, create) as the example repository, and `startServer` (config, module flags, open + migrate, then listen; a newer database refuses to start before the port is bound; `/healthz` now reads the database).
- Tests: 99 passed in total on rebased main (new: PRAGMAs, idempotent migrations and hook, failing migration leaves old schema, newer database refused by runner and by `startServer`, held lock blocks a second runner, repository).
- Deviations: migration SQL is hand-written to match 07 (Drizzle Kit generation is not wired; `drizzle-kit` is not installed, only `drizzle-orm`), so the SQL is the source of truth and `schema.ts` mirrors it; the 3.3 SQLite-version startup assertion is not in this PR text and is not done (bundled SQLite is 3.53.4 on this install); size is 404 changed lines counting `package.json`.

## PR 10 — [M2.3] Setup and login

- Branch `pr-10-setup-login`; squash commit on main `46d1aae`.
- Done (module `core`, all five operations are `core` in `x-modules`): `GET /setup/status`, `POST /setup` (one transaction: user, settings, `instance` row with a random install id; the transaction re-checks that no user exists, so concurrent requests cannot both win), `POST /auth/login`, `POST /auth/logout`, `POST /auth/password`. scrypt (N=32768, r=8, p=1, 16-byte salt, stored as `scrypt$salt$hash`, async, constant-time compare); opaque 256-bit session value, only its SHA-256 stored in `web_sessions` (migration `core/0002_web_sessions`), cookie `mw_session; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/`, sliding 30-day expiry; login failure limiter (5 per 15 minutes, per client IP and per account, in memory) answering 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`; unknown user runs a dummy scrypt so response and timing match a wrong password; non-GET cookie routes require `Origin` equal to the request host (`origin_mismatch`); password change deletes all other sessions.
- Tests: 105 passed in total on main (new `auth.test.ts`: second owner refused (409), wrong password and unknown user identical, logout and Origin check, expiry plus sliding renewal with an injected clock, brute-force limit per IP and per account with `Retry-After` and recovery after the window, password change ends other sessions). Size 400 changed lines excluding lockfile.
- Deviations/decisions: `POST /setup` returns `{"state":"ready"}` without `recovery_key`, and `GET /setup/status` never returns `needs_confirmation`, because the recovery key and `POST /setup/confirm` are out of scope until M3.2 (the OpenAPI response schema still lists `recovery_key`; the M3.2 PR closes the gap). The `RateLimit-*` headers on successful responses are not sent yet. The timezone is not checked against the IANA database (left for the settings PR). `request.ip` is used as the client IP; `trustProxy` must be set when the server sits behind Caddy (deployment PR), otherwise every client shares the proxy address. Password length is capped at 1024 characters (the spec gives only the minimum of 10). scrypt parameters are fixed in code; the hash prefix `scrypt$` allows a later parameter change.
- Not done (per PR text): recovery key, pairing, settings, `X-MileWage-*` response headers, `setup_incomplete` guard.

`apps/server` boots from config, applies module flags, opens SQLite with the 10 section 3.4 PRAGMAs, migrates (refusing a newer database), and serves health, setup and login under the error model of 08 section 7.1. `npm run verify` is green on Node 22.22.0.

Routes take an `AuthFn(request, 'S' | 'SD')` so the devices PR only swaps the function.

The `/odometer` year-reading routes are not in this PR text and are left for the logbook PRs. Size is about 450 changed lines, over the 400 cap (the test harness is 51 of them).

## PR M2.10 — DiagnosticsPort, event kinds, allowlist schemas, canary test

- Branch `pr-M2-10-diagnostics-port`. Done in `packages/shared/src/diagnostics/`: `DiagnosticsPort` (`record(kind, data)`) with a frozen `noopDiagnostics`; a small schema DSL (`int`, `bool`, `oneOf`, `str`, `ints`, `strs`, `obj`; undeclared fields rejected, strings only by pattern or enum); the envelope check `validateEvent` (kind, `at`, level, allowed `src` per kind, `seq`, `shift`/`cap` ids, `v`, then `data` against the kind's schema; reasons name a field path, never a value); the coordinate belt (decimal with 4+ places, standalone 9+ digit token) plus a credential belt (token prefixes, session cookie name, tailnet host suffix) on every string; `crashMessage` and the `msg` gate by class allowlist (16 section 4.3). Kinds in this PR: `service.event`, `track.window`, `crash`, `diag.self`.
- Tests: 114 passed in total (7 new in `packages/shared/test/diagnostics.test.ts`): every kind accepts its sample; canary strings (coordinates, E7 values, address, tailnet host, token prefixes) substituted into every string leaf are rejected or never reach the stored event; undeclared `lat`/`note`/`address` fields rejected at any depth; free text in enums rejected; non-integers in counts rejected; crash message gating and redaction; no-op port.
- Deviation: the 400-line cap forced a split. `app.start`, `upload.window`, `shift.summary`, `engine.run` (the rest of the A-rig set of 16 section 9.3) go in PR M2.10b, same PR text, before M2.11. Size is about 415 lines including this log entry.
- Decisions/limits: a payout canary (`12.34`, `1234`) and an alphanumeric `CANARY_NOTE` cannot be told from a version or a count by a pattern, so the defence is that no kind declares a money or label field and pattern fields stay narrow; the tests assert exactly that. Numbers are bounded integers, so a coordinate-sized count such as 436532000 is not refused (it is a legal count); only strings get the belt.
- Merged to main as `b806bd5` (verify green: 120 tests).

## PR M2.5 — Devices module

- Branch `pr-M2-5-devices`; squash commit on main `debdc3e`.
- Done (module `devices`, depends on `core`): `POST /pairing-tokens` (`mwp_` + 43 chars, 10 minutes, only the SHA-256 stored, `qr_payload` from `X-Forwarded-Proto/Host`), `POST /pairing/redeem` (public; token consumed and device created in one transaction; unknown, expired and used tokens give the same 401 `invalid_pairing_token`; 10 failures per 15 minutes per IP then 429 with `Retry-After`; 4 KiB body limit; returns `device_token` once plus `Settings`), `GET /devices`, `PATCH /devices/{id}`, `DELETE /devices/{id}` (idempotent revoke, 404 for unknown). Migration `devices/0001_devices`. New `Authenticator` (08 section 16.5): the devices module plugs in the `Authorization: Bearer mwd_...` branch; revoked gives 401 `device_revoked`, unknown or malformed gives 401 `auth_required`, `last_seen_at` is written at most once a minute. Scope matrix: `AuthFn(request, 'S' | 'SD')`; a device on an `S` route gets 403 `scope_forbidden`; bearer requests skip the Origin check. With the devices module off a bearer token is plain unauthenticated.
- Tests: 126 passed in total on rebased main (new `devices.test.ts`, 7 tests: QR payload, one-time and expired tokens, hashed storage, scope matrix over five write routes plus both read routes, revoke and rename and list, last-seen throttle, redeem limit per IP, module off).
- Decisions: `readSettings` (the `Settings` body returned at pairing) is a core service handed to the devices module, which does not import core routes. `app_version` is validated but not stored (no column in 07). `clock_skew_ms`/`online` on `Device` are optional and come with M2.8 / live. The `device.seen` event is not emitted yet (no listener exists). Size is about 450 changed lines, over the 400 cap.

## PR M2.6 — Shifts (core logbook), merged as two commits

- Branches `pr-M2-6-shifts` (main `4646b0d`) and `pr-M2-6b-shift-edit` (main `72d5534`). One PR text, two squash commits, because the whole scope came to about 620 changed lines against the 400 cap.
- Part 1: migration `core/0004_shifts` (07 section 2.3 shifts table; the phone-result columns `phone_total_m`, `phone_calc_version`, `input_digest`, `interruptions_json` are left out and arrive by `ALTER` with M5.3), `POST /shifts` (idempotent on `client_id`, case-insensitive: replay returns 200 with the row unchanged; one active shift per user, 409 `shift_already_active` carries `active_shift`; vehicle must exist; `started_at` more than 5 minutes ahead is 422; `segmenter` defaults from `stops_enabled`; `local_date` from the instance time zone), `GET /shifts` (newest first by `(started_at, id)`, opaque keyset cursor, `status`, `vehicle_id`, `from`, `to`, `limit` 1-200), `GET /shifts/{ref}` (integer id or UUID). A device sees only shifts started in the last 14 days or written to by it in that time (`device_touched_at`); others are 404 `shift_not_found`.
- Part 2: `PATCH /shifts/{ref}` (notes, vehicle, last-writer-wins on the client's `updated_at`; not applied answers 200 with the current row and `X-MileWage-Applied: false`; ties go to the server; finalized is 409), `POST /shifts/{ref}/end` (minimal: status and `ended_at`, idempotent, `manifest`/`phone_result`/`interruptions` accepted and ignored until M5.3, totals stay null), `PUT /shifts/{ref}/odometer/{kind}` (migration `core/0005_odometer`, upsert on (shift, kind), same last-writer rule on `recorded_at`).
- Tests: 136 passed in total on rebased main (new `shifts.test.ts`: idempotent start, one active, time-zone local date, end idempotency and ordering, last-writer-wins and ties, odometer upsert, paging and filters, the 14-day device window including the widening by a device write).
- Decisions: a new shift's `updated_at` is its `started_at` (not server time), so edits the phone made offline after starting are not rejected as older than the create. `ProblemError` gained an `extra` member map for `active_shift`. Not done (per PR text): finalize, purge, recalculate, trips, `/odometer` year readings, `shift.*` events, overwrite log (sync module). The `X-MileWage-Applied` header is only sent on the three last-writer-wins routes.
- Environment note: main briefly failed `typecheck:phone` after M4.2 (an `apps/phone` tsconfig issue from the phone agent); I waited for their fix commit `0db3832` instead of touching `apps/phone`.

## PR M2.7 — Tracking ingest

- Branch `pr-M2-7-telemetry`; squash commit on main `f66e51f`.
- Done (module `tracking`, depends on `core`, `devices`): migration `tracking/0001_telemetry` (`telemetry_points`, 07 section 2.5, `UNIQUE (device_id, seq)`), `POST /telemetry/batch` (device credential only: a session gets 403 `scope_forbidden`, none 401). Order as 08 section 5: auth, more than 500 points is 413 `payload_too_large`, shift resolved by `shift_client_id` in the device's 14-day window (404 `shift_not_found`, 409 `shift_finalized`), per-point refusal (`invalid_coordinate`, `future_timestamp` over 5 minutes ahead, `outside_shift_window` from start - 5 min to end + 10 min, or now + 5 min while active; poor accuracy is stored), then one transaction of `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (device_id, seq) DO NOTHING` giving `inserted` and `duplicates`. Unknown fields and wrong types are 422 for the whole batch. Response also carries `server_time` and `upload_hint_s` (fixed 60 until the live module exists). The write also sets `shifts.device_touched_at`. Logs carry only counts.
- gzip: a global `preParsing` hook (`src/gzip.ts`) accepts `Content-Encoding: gzip` on any route (other encodings: 415); compressed size is capped per route (`config.maxEncoded`, 256 KiB for telemetry), Fastify's body limit (1 MiB for telemetry, 64 KiB elsewhere) is enforced on the decompressed stream, corrupt gzip is 400 `malformed_request`.
- Tests: 142 passed in total on rebased main (new `tracking.test.ts`, 6 tests: replay stores once and mixed duplicates, partial refusal with codes, post-end window, 500 cap, decompression bomb, over-256 KiB, corrupt and unsupported encodings, auth and scope, finalized and unknown shift, module off). `AuthFn` gained the `'D'` (device only) scope.
- Not done (per PR text / later PRs): 503/507 guards (M3), telemetry rate limit (M2.8), late-point recompute and `telemetry.accepted`/live publish (M5, live), `X-Server-Time` header (M2.8), `trip_overrides` and `PUT .../trip-overrides` (the other `tracking` route, not in this PR text). Size about 410 changed lines.
## PR M4.3 — [M4.3] Trim the tracker

- Branch `pr-M4-3-trim-tracker`.
- Done in `apps/phone/native/comma-tracker`: deleted `ShiftOverlay.kt` and `BootReceiver.kt`; removed the overlay ticker, overlay and boot-restart code and the old module functions (`requestBatteryOptimizationExemption`, overlay permission, `setShiftTiming`, `consumeOpenConsole`, `setDistanceUnit`); added `requestBatteryOptimizationSettings` (opens the system settings list, needs no permission). Cadence comes from JS: `startTracking(cadence?)` parses `CadenceConfig` (interval 10 s, displacement 20 m, max delay 30 s, still timeout 150 s as defaults; ranges validated, invalid config returns false), stores it in SharedPreferences so a restarted service uses the same values. Module manifest declares no permissions; `apps/phone/app.json` lists exactly the six of 09 section 5.3, and `plugins/withManifestPolicy.js` (MileWage's version of Comma's permission-stripping plugin) adds `tools:node="remove"` for `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`, `RECORD_AUDIO`, `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS`. `COPIED-FROM-COMMA.md` records the changes.

Release build still succeeds (75.8 MB APK, 1m27s).

Fixed in the follow-up commit `0db3832` on main (untracked the build output, ignore rules, vitest excludes `apps/phone`). The build output remains in history of `04042a4` (no force-push).

## PR M2.8 — Rate limits, protocol headers, clock skew

- Branch `pr-M2-8-limits-headers`; squash commit on main `a710e14`.
- Done: `src/hooks.ts`. Every response (also 404, 401, 422, `/healthz`) carries `X-MileWage-Server` (from `apps/server/package.json`, so `version:check` covers it), `X-MileWage-Api: 1`, `X-Server-Time`, and, once setup has created the `instance` row, `X-MileWage-Instance` and `X-MileWage-Restore-Gen` (read per response, so a restore shows at once). Everything under `/api` also gets `Cache-Control: no-store`. Rate limits are in-memory token buckets keyed by the credential (`Authorization` or cookie header, else the client IP): `general` 300 per minute (burst 300) and `telemetry` 60 per minute with burst 30 (route `config.rateLimit`); exceeding answers 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`; all `/api` responses carry `RateLimit-Limit/-Remaining/-Reset` (closing the M2.3 gap). `/healthz` is not limited. Login and redeem keep their own failure limiters (M2.3, M2.5). Clock skew: the device branch records `X-Client-Time - server now` per device in memory (every observation, so it returns to about 0 when the driver fixes the clock; junk is ignored) and `GET /devices` shows it as `clock_skew_ms`; nothing is ever rewritten or blocked by skew; only the impossible is refused (more than 5 minutes ahead: 422 for entities, `future_timestamp` for points). `trustProxy`: new `MILEWAGE_TRUST_PROXY` (comma-separated addresses or CIDRs, or `true`; default none) passed to Fastify, so `request.ip` honours `X-Forwarded-For` only from Caddy. The compose file does not set it yet; the infra owner must set it to Caddy's network address (for the default compose network, its subnet) or login and redeem limits see only the proxy address.
- Tests: 148 passed in total on rebased main (new `protocol.test.ts`, 6 tests: headers on every kind of response and restore generation changes, no instance headers before setup, 300/min with 429 and refill and separate credentials, telemetry burst 30 then 429 and recovery, skew cases (ahead, behind, junk, fixed, never rewritten, 5-minute future refusal), trusted versus untrusted `X-Forwarded-For`). Size about 330 changed lines.
- Decisions: "300 per minute" is a bucket of 300 refilled at 5 per second, "60 per minute, burst 30" a bucket of 30 refilled at 1 per second (the phone's backlog drain therefore sees 429 with `Retry-After: 1` after 30 back-to-back batches and must honour it, which it does per 08 section 4.2). The spec's other limited routes (`POST /backups`, diagnostics, exports) are added with their routes. `tsconfig.base.json` gained `resolveJsonModule` for the version import.

## Summary after M2.4-M2.8 (server track)

`apps/server` now serves setup/login, settings with module flags, vehicles, devices (pairing, bearer auth, scopes), shifts (start, list, get, edit, minimal end, shift odometer) and telemetry ingest, with rate limits and protocol headers. `npm run verify` green on Node 22.22.0 at every merge. M3 (backup) and M4 (phone) can build on these.

## PR M4.4 — [M4.4] Native buffer DB and tracker interface (three commits, three pushes)

- Branch `pr-M4-4-buffer-interface`; it came to about 850 changed lines in one piece, so it was split to respect the 400-line cap into parts (main commits `101ca59`, `047244a`, and the one with this entry): 1/3 buffer logic and JUnit, 2/3 service and module wiring, 3/3 TS interface, fake and contract test.
- Done: `tracker.db` (separate file from the app DB) with `telemetry_buffer` and `telemetry_quarantine` exactly as 02 section 3.1 / 15 section 2.1; `TrackerBuffer` over a small `SqlDb` seam (Android SQLite in the app, plain JDBC in JUnit); the service now stores every fix unfiltered (no 20 m / 42 m/s filter, no running distance, no write to Comma's `comma.db`), converts to e7/metres/dm/s (`PointMapper`) and records `gnss_offset_ms`. Module API: `start(shiftClientId, cadence?)`, `stop`, `isTracking`, `readPending(limit)`, `markUploaded(seqs)` (sets `UPLOADED` + `uploaded_at`, keeps rows), `markRejected(seqs)` (to quarantine), `evictUploaded(olderThanMs)` (UPLOADED only), `gnssOffsetMs()` (null if the last fix is over 10 minutes old), `requestBatteryOptimizationSettings()`. TS side in `apps/phone/src/tracker`: `Tracker` interface, `FakeTracker` with the same semantics, lazy `createNativeTracker()`.
- Tests: JUnit 16 in total (7 buffer semantics: seq never reused, oldest first, unfiltered, markUploaded keeps rows, evict never touches PENDING, quarantine, 1000-seq chunking; 3 mapper; 6 cadence), run through `:comma-tracker:testReleaseUnitTest` (uses `org.xerial:sqlite-jdbc` as a test dependency). Jest: contract test (5 cases) against the fake plus 3 fake-specific tests. After the change the release build passes (75.8 MB) and the merged-manifest lint test ran for real against the build output (no forbidden permission).
- Decisions: `markRejected` stores the reason `server_rejected` (the JS signature has no reason, per 09). `tracker.db` has no `PRAGMA user_version` yet (additive migrations are not needed until a column is added). Unknown accuracy is stored as 9999 m (column is NOT NULL; the engine drops it at the 50 m filter). The interruption record, `readDiag` and capture methods of 09 section 5.1 are not in this PR text. The native module is not exercised on a device here; `createNativeTracker` has no automated test (needs the app runtime).

## PR M4.5 - Permission ladder

- Pure state machine in `apps/phone/src/permissions/ladder.ts`: disclosure (Play prominent disclosure, before any OS prompt), foreground location, background ("all the time", only after foreground), activity recognition, notifications, battery settings. `describeLadder` gives steps, next step, tracking mode (`full`, `while_using`, `none`) and warnings; a blocked step does not hold the ladder; `canStartShift` is true for `while_using` (with a warning) and false without location or with location services off. OS access is behind `PermissionsPort`; `FakePermissions` is the test double. New `PermissionsScreen` (Settings, Permissions) and a `Services` context (`src/services.tsx`) with `tracker` and `permissions`; `App` takes optional `services`.
- Tests: 8 new jest tests (7 state machine, 1 screen walk through to battery settings). 26 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- NOT wired: the real `PermissionsPort` (expo-location, expo-notifications / PermissionsAndroid) is not installed or written; the app uses `notWiredPermissions`, which asks nothing. Nothing ran on a phone. No warning colour exists in the tokens, so warnings use the text colour.

## PR M1.4 — Stage 4 gaps, unfinished shifts, clock flags (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine`: `gaps.ts` with `detectGaps` (`gap_counted`, `gap_large`, `gap_dropped`, plus the chord metres counted across gaps), `interruptionFlags` (`tracking_interrupted`), `clockSkewFlag` and `medianMs` (`clock_skew` beyond 2 minutes), `qualityFlags` (`poor_accuracy_share` above 30%, `low_fix_density` below one accepted fix per 5 minutes), `sortFlags` (fixed order `(code, time, detail)`), `lastFixEnd` (the end the app offers for an unfinished shift, 14 section 7.4). `gate()` now also returns `segments` and `spikes` (additive; totals and the golden outputs are unchanged, so no `algorithmVersion` bump). `ShiftInput` gained optional `autoClosed` and `clockSkewMs` (the host measures the median, the engine reads no clock).
- Tests: 64 passed in the engine package (13 new in `gaps.test.ts`: rest with GPS asleep is no gap, tunnel, killed app with interruption, dead battery, spike across a gap, thresholds). Scenario builders in `test/support/scenarios.ts`.
- Interpretation, not in the spec text: a gap's time step is the silence before the segment's end fix (time since the previous plausible fix, where a fix inside the 20 m gate counts as signal), not the span since the anchor. Read literally, every first step after a long rest would be a gap, because the anchor does not move during the rest; 14 section 7.1 says a rest is not a gap, so the engine follows the intent.
- Not in this PR: `shift_auto_closed` and the other flags are emitted by `processShift` (M1.5).

## PR M2.11 — Minimal diagnostics.db and POST /diagnostics/events (Server track)

- Done (module `diagnostics`, depends on `core`, on by default): `diagnostics.db` as its own file in the data directory with its own connection and migration folder (`src/diagnostics/diagnostics-migrations/diagnostics`, copied next to the bundle by `build-server.mjs` and into the image). Table `diag_events` and its four indexes exactly as 16 section 7.1; `diag_daily` and the capture tables are not created yet. A corrupt or newer-schema file is renamed aside (`diagnostics.db.bad-<ms>`, plus `-wal`/`-shm`) and recreated, with a warning line, never fatal (D15). The file is opened on first use, so with the module off no file exists. `POST /diagnostics/events` (S or D): each event goes through the shared `validateEvent`; rejects are listed by index with a reason in the 08 enum and never fail the batch; a device may send `phone_*` sources (with `seq`), a web session only `web`; idempotent on `(device_id, src, seq)`; `expires_at` basic +90 d, verbose +14 d, `crash` +365 d. Settings level `off` stores nothing (counted as `discarded`), `verbose` events are kept only while the level is verbose and not expired. Limits: 256 KiB compressed, 1 MiB, 200 events (422 beyond), new rate class `diagnostics` 30 per minute, burst 15, per credential. `buildApp` now starts module jobs and stops them on close (the store closes this way).
- Tests: 156 passed in total (new `diagnostics.test.ts`, 8 tests: replay and gzip, per-event rejects with reasons, coordinate-like version refused, auth/limits/scope, level setting including the 7-day revert, rate limit, module off creates no file, corrupt file renamed aside).

The server's own events (`DiagnosticsPort` real implementation), `diag.self`, the retention pruning job, size caps and the low-disk discard (`disk_low`) are not done; `discarded` here covers level `off` and verbose-while-basic, not disk. The reject `reason` is mapped from the shared validator's text by prefix. The `seq` strictly-increasing rule is not enforced beyond uniqueness.

- Part 1: `src/api/client.ts` (`ApiClient`: injected fetch, `X-Client-Time` on every request, bearer for devices, Problem errors as `ApiError` with `code`, `retryable`, `Retry-After`; no logging), `src/pairing/pairing.ts` (`parsePairingPayload` for `milewage://pair?v=1&u=..&t=mwp_..` (08 2.4); `Pairing.redeem` posts `/pairing/redeem`, stores the credential (device id, `mwd_` token, base URL, `X-MileWage-Instance`) in the secure `KeyValueStore` and the settings in the plain one; `refreshSettings` (`GET /settings`, cache kept on failure, error returned so a 401 can mean re-pair); `unpair` keeps the telemetry buffer; `deriveFlags`: tracking, stops (module AND `stops_enabled`), segmenter, diagnostics, unit). Test support: `test/support/mock.ts` (mock server recording calls, fakes for services).

Only the manual paste path works. The two parts together are about 700 lines against the 400 cap; the split is why they are two commits.

## PR M1.5 — Route simplification, inputDigest, processShift (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine`: `route.ts` (own integer-input RDP in a local equirectangular projection, 10 m tolerance, first and last points kept, timestamps carried; the only transcendental call is one `cos`, rounded to 1e-6 before use), `digest.ts` (`inputDigest`: 64-bit FNV-1a over a canonical text of the normalized fixes, window, segmenter, labels, interruptions, edits; done with two 32-bit halves, no BigInt, checked against a BigInt reference), `trips.ts` (`makeTripKey`, the whole-shift segmenter, trip metres by cumulative rounding so trips add up to `totalM` exactly), `canonical.ts` (`canonicalJson`: sorted keys, integers only), `process.ts` (`processShift`: stages 1-4, whole-shift segment, 8, flags in fixed order, stats incl. `gap_m`). `segmenter: 'stops'` behaves as `whole_shift` until M6 (it cannot change a total). Stage 7 (edits) is M1.6. `shift_auto_closed` comes from the new `ShiftInput.autoClosed`.
- Golden: `test/fixtures/golden-shift.json` holds the full `processShift` output for the 4 rides and 5 scenarios, with its own append-only ledger (sha256 of the canonical JSON per `algorithmVersion`). Same guard rules as `golden.json`: changed outputs without a bump fail. `algorithmVersion` stays 1 (new files only, no existing output changed).
- Tests: 100 passed in the engine package (36 new). Properties on every fixture: shuffle and duplicates change nothing and the digest is equal; extra fixes inside 20 m change no total, trip or route; trips sum to `totalM`; total = active + deadhead + personal; no clock dependence (fake timers); 25 seeded random walks. Performance: 5,000 fixes, median of 9 runs under 50 ms asserted; measured median 14.6 ms, max 21.4 ms over 20 runs on `coder` (Node 22.22, JIT warm). Not measured: Hermes / the S24 Ultra (budget 200 ms).
- Not done: the equivalence check against `simplify-js` named in 14 section 5.5 (the package is not installed and adding a dependency was out of scope; the RDP is checked against hand-computed cases instead). Mark it open for the Spike A device run.

## PR M2.9b — MILEWAGE_TRUST_PROXY in the compose stack (Server track)

- Done: `infra/docker/docker-compose.yml` gives the stack a fixed network `172.29.88.0/24` (override with `MILEWAGE_PROXY_SUBNET`; the smoke script assumes a /24) and sets `MILEWAGE_TRUST_PROXY` on the api container to that subnet, so the API believes `X-Forwarded-For` only from Caddy (the network holds only Caddy and the API). The image also copies `diagnostics-migrations` (M2.11).
- Smoke (`infra/docker/smoke.sh`, ran for real here with Docker 29.1.3, exit 0): the api container sees `MILEWAGE_TRUST_PROXY` equal to the subnet; the Caddy and api container addresses lie inside it; the diagnostics migration file is in the image; eleven failed `pairing/redeem` calls from inside the container (127.0.0.1, not trusted) with eleven different `X-Forwarded-For` values end in 429, i.e. an untrusted source cannot pick its own client address.
- Unverified: that Caddy forwards the real client address as `X-Forwarded-For` and the API then uses it for limits (needs a request that arrives through Caddy from a non-loopback source, e.g. the VM test D6 with Tailscale; 10 section 8 says Spike D must confirm the source address Caddy sees). Caddyfile `trusted_proxies` is not set (not needed while Caddy is the edge on loopback).

## PR M1.6 — Stage 7 trip edits, trip_key, override orphans (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine`: `edits.ts` (`applyEdits`, `sortEdits`) wired into `processShift` as stage 7. An edit is `{tripKey, kind?, purpose?}`; it changes only `kind` and `purpose` (never `originalKind`, times or distance), and totals are recomputed from the final kinds. An edit whose key matches no trip is not applied, not dropped and not moved to a nearby trip: it comes back as an `override_orphaned` flag whose `detail` is the tripKey, once per key. `trip_key` is `<shiftClientId>:<segment start ms>:<original kind>` (`makeTripKey`, from M1.5). Edits are applied in a fixed sorted order (the same order as in `inputDigest`), so the order the host passes them in cannot change the result; two edits for one key merge field by field, the later in that order wins.
- Tests: 109 passed in the engine package (9 new): key format and stability under reorder/duplicates, personal reclassification keeps metres, purpose-only edit, edit survives a recompute with later fixes, orphan by wrong original kind or wrong start, an earlier late fix that moves the trip start orphans the edit (no guessing), edit order irrelevant, multi-trip case with exact cumulative rounding. Golden outputs unchanged (no edits in the fixtures), so no `algorithmVersion` bump.
- Note for M6: with the stop-based segmenter a label can move trip boundaries, so orphans become likelier; the web warning in 14 section 6.7 is a UI item, not engine.

## PR M1.7 — Canonical-JSON conformance runner, Comma sanity-check script (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine`: `scripts/conformance.ts` (+ `test/support/conformance.ts`): `check` runs every golden fixture through `processShift` twice, compares the canonical JSON byte for byte, and compares with the committed `golden-shift.json`; `emit-inputs`, `emit-outputs` and `compare` are the Hermes path (emit the inputs as one JSON file, run them on the device with the same engine source, bring back the outputs file, `compare` the two). `scripts/comma-sanity.ts` (+ `test/support/comma.ts`): reads `[{id, routePath, trackedMileage}]` (routePath as Comma stores it: JSON points or a Google encoded polyline), sums the engine's haversine over the route and compares with the stored total (`--unit km|mi`), exit 1 if a shift is outside the limits. Run with `npm run conformance -w @milewage/track-engine -- check` and `npm run comma-sanity -w @milewage/track-engine -- <export.json>` (both use vite-node from the root install).
- IMPORTANT, not verified: the owner's Comma export was NOT available. The sanity script was built and tested only on synthetic Comma-shaped data (engine rides turned into a 10 m simplified route plus a `toFixed(2)` total, in both JSON and polyline form). The limits (route at most 4% shorter and 1% longer than the stored total, shifts under 1 km skipped) are guesses; they must be reviewed against the first real export. Which unit Comma's `trackedMileage` is stored in (display unit per settings) also needs the owner to say; `--unit` must match. The M1.7 roadmap exit "Comma sanity check agrees" is therefore OPEN. Also open: the Hermes half of the conformance run (needs the S24 Ultra, Spike A); only the Node half exists.
- Tests: 124 passed in the engine package (15 new): repeat runs and golden byte-identical, device-file path lossless, compare naming the first differing byte, tampered golden detected, no floats or undefined in result bytes, polyline round trip and key spellings, synthetic shifts within the limits (JSON and polyline), simplified route never longer than the path, gross errors caught (inflated total, miles read as km, no route, tiny shift), CLI exit codes. Both scripts were also run once for real: `check` printed OK for 9 fixtures; the sanity script exited 1 on a deliberately wrong hand-made row.
- M1 exit status: properties pass, fixtures committed with the version-bump guard (`golden.json`, `golden-shift.json`), 5,000 fixes at a 14.6 ms median on Node; Comma agreement and Hermes byte-identity still open as above.

## PR M3.1 — Backup engine (Server track)

- Done (module `backup`, depends on `core`, **not toggleable**): migration `backup/0001_backup_runs` (07 section 2.1). `src/backup/engine.ts`: `db.backup()` on the one write connection in chunks of 256 pages into `<backups>/.tmp/`, then on the copy opened read-only `PRAGMA integrity_check`, `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`, and a row-count comparison (`shifts`, `stop_labels` if the table exists) against the live counts taken at the start (a copy with fewer rows fails); then gzip with a SHA-256 over the final bytes, written with mode 0600 and moved into place with an atomic `rename`. A `BackupCodec` seam (`suffix`, `transforms()`) is where the age encryptor of M3.2 plugs in; M3.1 files are `milewage-<UTC stamp with ms>.db.gz`. `pre_migration` and `pre_restore` runs go to `pre-migration/` and `pre-restore/`. `src/backup/service.ts`: the one service function (single flight, records `backup_runs`, prunes only after a verified backup; a failed run deletes nothing, keeps `lastFailure`, and writes no row). `retention.ts`: 7 daily (newest file of each of the last 7 days that have one), 4 Sundays, 12 first-of-month days by the owner's time zone, last 3 pre-migration, last 2 pre-restore. `schedule.ts`: `nextRunAt` (local 03:30, DST-safe, tested across both Chicago changes) and the nightly job (`backupSchedule: true` only in `start.ts`; tests leave it off). `GET /backups`, `POST /backups` (session only; 1 per 15 minutes for manual and shift-end runs, 429 `backup_rate_limited` with `Retry-After`; 201 with the `Backup` object). New config `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DIR` (default `backups`, `/app/backups` in the image).
- Tests: 14 new in `backup.test.ts` (round trip with hash and gunzip and integrity of the stored file; a copy that lost its shifts is rejected with no file, no row and older backups intact; a damaged page is rejected; 30,000 rows on the same connection with writes continuing while the backup runs; pre-migration 3 and pre-restore 2; tiers; no owner means no backup; retention calendar over 400 days of files with the exact expected set; Los Angeles day boundary; foreign files untouched; schedule across DST; the two routes, the limit and auth/scope).
- Deviations / not done: about 780 lines changed (262 of them tests), roughly twice the 400 cap; kept in one commit because engine, service and routes share their tests. Not in M3.1: encryption (M3.2), `GET /backups/{id}/download` and `PUT /backups/offsite-ack` (they belong with the off-site state; not in any M3 PR text, still open), the `shift.ended` trigger (the event is not emitted yet; the `shift_end` trigger value and the 15-minute limit already exist), the disk-space backup skip (M3.5). The `integrity_check` runs synchronously on the main thread; for the 1 GB drill (B9) the worker-thread fallback of the roadmap is not built. Whether a 15-minute-stall-free backup works under the B3 load is for the VM (B5, B5b): not measured here.

## PR M4.7 - Start / End shift (two commits: controller, then screen)

- Part 1: `src/shift/controller.ts` (`ShiftController`): start records first (tracker `start(clientId)` before any network call; with the `tracking` module off the tracker is not started), then `POST /shifts` idempotent on `client_id` with `segmenter` from the cached flags, then the start odometer `PUT`. Unacknowledged calls are kept in the persisted `ActiveShift` and finished by `sync()` (create is retried with the same client_id; start odometer; end with the same `ended_at`). 409 `shift_already_active` stops the tracker and returns the server shift; `adopt` continues it. 409 on end (finalized) is terminal. `src/shift/feed.ts` (`PointFeed`): the single reader of `readPending`, announcing new seqs to the live accumulator so markUploaded in M4.8 cannot hide points from it; `createLiveAccumulator` from `@milewage/track-engine` (new workspace dependency of apps/phone) gives the running metres. `Pairing.session()`, `createServices()` (object graph), test kit `test/support/shiftKit.ts`.
- Part 2: Home screen: vehicle (first one), start odometer (display unit), Start shift (refused with a message when location permission is missing), live distance refreshed every 5 s, odometer edit, End shift, "not yet sent" notes, continue-server-shift prompt.
- Tests: 12 controller tests + 3 screen tests. 57 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- Limits: the running distance after an app restart only counts points still PENDING (the Tracker interface has no read-by-shift of UPLOADED rows); it is shown as approximate and the server total is the figure that counts. The end odometer is sent once and not queued (outbox arrives with M5.1). End does not yet wait for point upload: M4.8 adds the drain-before-end barrier. A 409 on a retried create in `sync()` drops the local shift without asking. Vehicle choice is the first vehicle (no picker). Active shift state sits in the plain KV store, which in production is `MemoryStore` (not wired to disk), so it does not survive a real restart yet. Nothing ran on a phone.

## PR M6.1 — Engine stage 5 stop detection (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine/src/stops.ts`: `detectStops` over the qualified fixes (stage 2 output, before the 20 m gate): one left-to-right scan, cluster within 40 m of the anchor, wake-up rule (fix after a gap of 90 s or more within 150 m is the cluster's last member), 120 s minimum dwell, merge rule (anchors within 100 m and under 120 s apart), start guard (flag `startGuarded`), `stopConfidence` (low 120-179 s, medium 180-599 s, high 600 s+), `makeStopKey` (`<client id>:<anchor ms>:stop`). `processShift` lists the stops when `segmenter` is `'stops'`; roles stay `none` and trips stay the whole-shift trip until M6.2. `algorithmVersion` is now 2 (output for `'stops'` inputs changed; the guard forced fixtures regeneration and refused to reuse version 1). Ledgers in `golden.json` and `golden-shift.json` keep the version 1 entries.
- Every constant is a named, documented GUESS (`STOP_*` in `stops.ts`) until Spike A13 / experiments E9, E10 measure them against the platform order history. None of these values has been validated on real rides. Thresholds are inclusive at the spec's edge (119 s no, 120 s yes).
- Tests: 141 passed in the engine package (13 new in `stops.test.ts`): delivery run (two real stops, red light ignored, 35 minute stop), dwell edge 119/120 s, confidence bands, queue creep as the documented false positive, wake-up at 100 m and not at 200 m, merge at 80 m and not at 150 m, start guard, jitter inside the gate still counts, tunnel is not a stop, poor-accuracy fixes ignored, shuffle/duplicates stable, `total_m` equal under both segmenters on every fixture, 5,000-fixes-with-stops under 50 ms.
- Size: about 430 changed lines with tests and scenario builders (fixtures excluded), slightly over the 400 cap.

## PR M6.2 — Engine stage 6 estimate: roles, split, labels (engine track)

- Done in `packages/track-engine/src/stops.ts` and `process.ts`: `estimateRoles` (state machine: starts deadhead; an unlabelled stop of 180 s up to under 30 min, not start-guarded, flips the state, pickup when it was deadhead and dropoff when active; a driver label wins: pickup sets active, dropoff sets deadhead, other changes nothing), `stopTrips` (the stop-based segmenter: each accepted segment takes the state in force at its end time, the state changes at the stop's departure, a trip is cut only where the state changes, purposes `Delivery (estimated)` and `Between deliveries (estimated)`), fallback to the whole-shift trip with flag `no_stops_detected` when no stop changed the estimate, flags `long_stop` (30 min or more) and `label_orphaned` (a label whose stop key no longer exists is neither applied nor moved). New constants `STOP_TOGGLE_DWELL_MS` (180 s) and `STOP_BREAK_DWELL_MS` (30 min), also documented guesses until Spike A13. `algorithmVersion` is now 3; ledgers keep entries 1 and 2.
- The split is an ESTIMATE on an assumption (one order at a time). It is unvalidated: no real ride has been run through it. Stacked orders are the known wrong case and are covered by a test that shows the estimate under-stating active metres and the labels (pickup, pickup, dropoff, dropoff) fixing it. `total_m` is asserted equal under both segmenters on every fixture, under every label set tried, and on 20 random stop-and-go shifts.
- Interpretation: `estRole` is what the engine would say for that stop in the state in force at that moment (so after a label it can differ from an unlabelled run); `role` is the label if any, else `estRole`. The spec does not say which; this choice lets one label correct the rest of the shift (14 section 6.6).
- Tests: 154 passed in the engine package (13 new on top of M6.1): toggle edges 179/180 s and 1799/1800 s, the estimate on the delivery run (deadhead, active, deadhead; active about 2,000 m of 6,300), stop at end of shift, start guard gives no pickup, stacked orders, labels incl. `other` and a pickup label on a low-dwell stop, orphaned label, label elsewhere keeps keys stable, label that moves a flip can orphan a trip edit (flagged), random stop-and-go invariants (trips sum to total, kinds partition the total, stops never overlap).
- Size: about 330 changed lines with tests.

## PR M4.8 - Upload worker

- `src/upload/worker.ts` (`UploadWorker`) reads pending points through the shared `PointFeed`, groups by shift (oldest first), sends gzip JSON (`fflate`, new dependency of apps/phone; Hermes has no CompressionStream) to `POST /telemetry/batch` with `Content-Encoding: gzip`: 100 points per batch, 500 per batch for a backlog above 500, never more than 500. Idempotent by identity: a lost reply resends the same seqs and the server answers duplicates; acknowledged = everything not in `rejected`, which goes to `markUploaded`; `rejected` goes to `markRejected` (quarantine); `evictUploaded` runs after a clean drain (14 days). Backoff 2/4/8/16/32/60 s +-20 percent, reset on success, `Retry-After` overrides. Responses per 08 4.3: 401 `device_revoked` -> state `repair` (halts, buffer kept, `resume()` after pairing, wired in Settings), other 401/403 -> `auth_problem`; 404 `shift_not_found` -> flush the outbox (`ShiftController.sync`) and retry, park the shift (not drop) after 3; 409 `shift_finalized` -> quarantine; 413 -> halve; 422 -> halve down to the single bad point and quarantine it; 429/503/507/5xx/network -> backoff. `upload_hint_s` clamped to 10-300 s. Create before upload (a shift whose create is unacknowledged sends nothing: `waiting_create`). `tracking` module off: nothing is sent. Barrier: `ShiftController.sync` does not send `shift.end` until `drainShift` shows no PENDING point of that shift (wired through `createServices`). `takeStats()` keeps the counters M4.9 turns into `upload.window`. Home screen starts/stops the loop when paired and shows a re-pair message.
- Tests: 20 jest tests in `test/upload.test.ts` (batching and cap, idempotent replay, rejected, hint clamp, counters, offline queue with growing backoff, 429/507, revoke and resume, 401/403, 404 park, 409, 413, 422 isolation, tracking off, not paired, create-before-upload, end barrier order, timer loop under fake timers). 77 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- Deviations / not done: about 900 changed lines in one commit (cap is 400; the worker, its wiring and its tests did not separate cleanly). The loop is a JS `setTimeout`: it stops when the OS freezes the JS runtime with the screen off. R4 (JS timers die screen-off, 17 section 11) is NOT assessed here and needs the A-rig check on the S24 Ultra; a native uploader is the documented fallback. No reachability-triggered wake (NetInfo not installed): after an outage the loop waits for its next retry delay (max 60 s). `shift.end` manifest, `phone_result` and interruptions are not sent (M5.3).

## PR M3.2 — Backup encryption with age, recovery key at setup, POST /setup/confirm (Server track)

- Done: `age-encryption` pinned at exactly 0.3.1 in `apps/server/package.json` (lockfile adds it plus the `@noble/*` and `@scure/base` packages it needs; pre-1.0 note in `src/backup/age.ts`). Every backup is now gzip then age to the public key in `config/secrets.json` (mode 0600, written atomically), files `*.db.gz.age`; with no key a backup fails (`BackupError` reason `no_key`) and no plain file is ever written. Plain X25519 identity (`generateX25519Identity`), not the hybrid post-quantum one, so the Go `age` and `rage` tools read the files. `POST /setup` now generates the keypair, stores only the public key and returns `{recovery_key, state: "needs_confirmation"}`; `GET /setup/status` reports `needs_setup | needs_confirmation | ready`; `POST /setup/confirm {last6}` (session) checks the typed tail case-insensitively, then marks the instance ready (new column `instance.setup_confirmed_at`, migration `core/0006`, which marks any pre-existing instance as confirmed) and removes the check from `secrets.json`. Until confirmed a hook in core answers `403 setup_incomplete` on every route except `/setup`, `/setup/status`, `/setup/confirm`, `/setup/restore`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/logout` and `/healthz`; nightly and shift-end backups wait for confirmation (`ready` hook on the service). The engine's codec is now chosen per run (`secretsCodec`), the gzip-then-seal pipeline surfaces errors from both stages.
- Tests: 262 pass in total on this tree (new `setup.test.ts`, 10 tests: key shown once and absent from config and from the DB and WAL files, the 403 gate and its open routes, sign out then back in then confirm, wrong tail 422 / case-insensitive / auth / idempotent, scheduled backups wait, encrypt-decrypt round trip with magic header, wrong key fails, no key writes nothing, `recipientOf` validation, and the cross-tool test). The cross-tool test (B14) runs the Go `age` tool 1.1.1 (Debian bookworm, copied out of a container) both ways: it decrypts our backup into a database that passes `integrity_check`, and our code decrypts a file the Go tool encrypted to our public key. It is `skipIf` the `age` binary is missing (`AGE_BIN` or `age` on PATH), so plain `npm run verify` on a machine without it runs 261 and skips that one; CI and `infra/vm-tests` (M3.6) need `age` installed to exercise it.
- Decisions / deviations: the server must check the typed tail without keeping the key, so `secrets.json` holds `key_tail_check`, a SHA-256 of the last 6 characters (they are the bech32 checksum of the key, so they reveal nothing usable), until the driver confirms; if the file is lost before confirmation the setup cannot be confirmed (restore from backup is not possible either, nothing exists yet). `/auth/login` is added to the spec's list of routes open before confirmation (a driver who signs out early would otherwise be locked out). Existing M2 tests were adapted: the harness `signUp()` now also confirms, `auth.test.ts` got a `configDir`, one protocol test advances the clock to refill the bucket the extra confirm call used. Size is about 450 lines of source and tests together beyond what M3.1 had, over the 400 cap. Still not done from the backup module: `GET /backups/{id}/download` and `PUT /backups/offsite-ack`, so `modules.backup_download` stays `false` in `GET /settings` until they exist.
- Unverified: decryption of a very large backup (memory use of the web-stream adapters) is not measured; B14 on the VM is still to be run by hand with the VM's `age` package.

## PR M4.9 - Phone diagnostics (JS side)

- `src/diagnostics/phoneDiagnostics.ts` (`PhoneDiagnostics`, implements the shared `DiagnosticsPort`): every event is built as an envelope (`src=phone_js`, strictly increasing `seq`, shift id, app version) and passed through `validateEvent` from `packages/shared` before it enters the ring; a rejected event is only counted (`schema_reject`), never logged or stored. Levels `off`/`basic`/`verbose` from the cached `GET /settings` (`diagnostics.level`, `verbose_until` enforced with the phone clock, `modules.diagnostics=false` behaves as off); off or module off deletes unsent rows. Producers: `app.start` (versions, device), `upload.window` (from `UploadWorker.takeStats()`, p95), `crash` (class by pattern, frames reduced to function names, message only for own/network classes and then redacted with the shared `crashMessage`), `diag.self` (dropped counters). Global JS error handler records fatal/non-fatal crashes and calls the previous handler. Ring cap 8000 with the 16 6.3 drop order; persisted to the plain store. `flush` sends up to 200 events gzip to `POST /diagnostics/events`, only when no telemetry is pending (tax data first), deletes what was sent, 404 = module off, 422 drops the batch, 5xx/429/network keeps it. `Services.startDiagnostics` / `diagnosticsWindow` (10-minute interval from the Home screen), `Pairing.onSettings` feeds the level. Settings shows level and events waiting (read only).
- Tests: 14 in `test/diagnostics.test.ts`, including the canary (hostile coordinates, E7 values, street address, `mwd_` token, tailnet host in model, version, frames, crash class and message; none reaches the ring, everything stored re-validates, own-class messages are redacted). 91 pass, 1 skipped in total. No `packages/shared` change was needed.
- Not done / unverified: `service.event`, `track.window` and the native `diag.*` tables are Kotlin work (09 5.4, not in this PR text); `shift.summary` needs battery start/end levels from native, so it is not emitted rather than invented; `engine.run` waits for the engine host in M5.3; `settings.applied` is not in the shared registry. The ring is memory-backed in production until a durable store is wired (same as pairing). No ride has produced real events yet; nothing ran on a phone.
## PR M7.1 — Web skeleton: Vite + React, Tailwind tokens, typed API client, auth shell (Web track)

- Done in `apps/web` (workspace `@milewage/web`): Vite 7 + React 19.2.3 (same pin as the phone, one copy in the tree) + react-router 7 + Tailwind 3. Colours and fonts come only from `packages/shared/src/tokens.ts`: `tailwind.config.ts` turns them into CSS variables (dark default, light under `prefers-color-scheme`). Fonts are self-hosted through `@fontsource` (no third-party request). `src/api/client.ts`: `openapi-fetch` typed by `paths` from `packages/shared/generated/api-types.ts`, `ApiError` from the problem document, `unwrap`, `describe`. `src/session.tsx`: `GET /setup/status` then `GET /settings`; 401 means signed out; `ready` carries settings and `modules`. `src/screens.tsx`: one list of screens with a per-screen module rule, used for both navigation and routes, so a hidden screen is also unreachable by URL (05 section 5).
- Root changes (needed): `tsconfig.json` excludes `apps/web` (own tsconfig with DOM and JSX), `vitest.config.ts` also picks up `*.test.tsx`, `package.json` gets `typecheck:web`, `build:web` and `typecheck:web` inside `verify`. Web tests choose jsdom per file with a `// @vitest-environment jsdom` docblock.
- Tests: 5 (shell states: signed in, 401, needs setup, server unreachable; the client rejects unknown paths and wrong bodies at compile time via `@ts-expect-error`, enforced by `typecheck:web`).
- Dev: `npm run dev -w apps/web` proxies `/api` to 127.0.0.1:3000 without `changeOrigin` (the server compares Origin with Host on writes). `npm run build:web` builds `apps/web/dist` (not committed). Not run in a browser.


## PR M4.10 - Hermes conformance harness and timing (A10, A12)

- `src/conformance/harness.ts`: `runConformance` runs every fixture input through `processShift` on the current runtime and compares `canonicalJson` of the result byte for byte with the text Node produced (`src/conformance/fixtures.json`: the engine golden inputs, 14 fixtures at algorithmVersion 3, generated by `npm run conformance:fixtures -w apps/phone` from `scripts/gen-conformance-fixtures.ts`). It names the first differing character and an excerpt, fails on an algorithmVersion mismatch and on fixtures missing either side. A12: warm-up plus 5 timed runs of `processShift` on a deterministic 5,000-fix ride (integer-only generator), reports min/median/max against the 200 ms bar. It also records raw `Math.sin/cos/atan2/sqrt` results and whether `HermesInternal` exists, so a mismatch can be explained. `ConformanceScreen` (Settings, Engine check, Run engine check) shows the report as selectable text.
- Tests: 8 jest tests (fixtures are current against the engine golden file and `goldenInputs()`; passes on Node; a one-unit difference is caught and located; version and missing fixture mismatch; Math probe; deterministic timing input; timing statistics with an injected clock; screen). 99 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- **UNRUN on Hermes / a device.** The jest run proves the harness and the Node side only (jest-expo runs on Node/V8). A10 and A12 are only answered by pressing "Run engine check" in a release build on the S24 Ultra, and the result must be pasted into the spike results; if A10 prints FAIL, the fallback in 14 section 10.1 applies (deterministic trig, bump `algorithmVersion`). The release build was not rebuilt here. No `packages/` change was needed.

## PR M3.3 — Restore with safety copy and automatic revert, POST /setup/restore (Server track)

- Done: `src/backup/restore.ts` (`createRestoreService`) and `POST /setup/restore` (multipart `file` + `recovery_key`, streamed to disk, 512 MiB, public but 409 `setup_already_done` once a user exists). Sequence: single-flight guard and server mode `restoring` (new `src/state.ts`; a hook answers every other `/api` request with 503 `restoring`, `Retry-After: 5`, except `/setup/status`) → decrypt with the recovery key and gunzip into `.restore-tmp/` → preflight on the copy (`integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check`, **abort without touching live data** if `schema_migrations` names a migration this build does not know, or on a wrong key or damaged file, which look the same to the caller: 422 `/recovery_key`) → an older backup is migrated forward **in the scratch copy** → safety copy of the live database as a `pre_restore` backup (skipped on a fresh host; if it fails the restore stops) → swap → verification output → resume. `RestoreVerification` has the 08 fields plus `instance_id` and `restore_generation`; the generation becomes `max(live before, restored) + 1` and the restored `instance_id` is kept. On a fresh host `config/secrets.json` is written from the key the driver supplied, so later backups open with the card they hold. Failed restores count against a limit of 5 per 15 minutes per client (429 with `Retry-After`). Caddy: the restore path gets `request_body` 512MB, every other API path keeps 2MB (limits moved from the site block into the handlers because the site-level limit capped the restore route as well); `infra/docker/smoke.sh` now uploads 3 MB of multipart through Caddy to the restore route and expects the API's 422, and passed (Docker 29.1.3).
- Deviation from `10` section 7 (on purpose, stated): the swap is **not** a file swap. The server holds one write connection and every repository captured it, so closing and reopening would leave dead handles. The verified, forward-migrated copy is `ATTACH`ed to the live connection and every table's rows are replaced in one transaction (foreign keys off for the copy, then `foreign_key_check` and `integrity_check` inside the transaction). The "auto-revert" is therefore a rollback: any failure, including the `verify` test seam, leaves the live database byte-for-byte logically unchanged, and the pre-restore backup remains on disk as the second safety net. Cost: for a very large database the whole replacement runs in one transaction (WAL grows to about the size of the data and the event loop is held; B8/B9 on the VM must show the time). The command-line `milewage-cli restore` for an instance that already has data is not built: the service supports it (`allowExisting`), but there is no CLI process or local control channel yet and no PR text asks for one.
- Tests: 10 new in `restore.test.ts` (fresh-host round trip through real multipart: counts, date range, generation 3 from 0 and 2, instance id and `X-MileWage-*` headers, login with the old owner, secrets written; 409 when a user exists; wrong key 422 ×5 then 429 and the host stays empty; damaged file, bad key text, missing key, non-multipart; **newer schema aborts**; an older backup (built from migrations without 0006) is migrated forward and restored; 503 `restoring` and the open status route; over live data a forced verification failure rolls back and leaves the safety copy; a successful restore over live data gives generation 8 from a live 7; the service refuses a populated instance without `allowExisting`).
- Unverified: restore time and memory for a ~1 GB database (B8), behaviour with phones re-sending after a generation change (M5), the Caddy 512 MB limit with a real 512 MiB upload.

Save sends only the changed fields (`changes()`), shows the server's returned settings (write and readback through the session) or the first field error, and stays disabled while nothing differs.

- Limits: the stops switch is always shown, because `modules.stops` is the effective flag (module and setting together) and cannot say whether the module is registered when the setting is off; with the module absent the switch would do nothing. No password-change form (`POST /auth/password` exists; not in the M7 table). Not run in a browser.


## PR M4.11a - Android permissions adapter (Phone adapters)

- `apps/phone/src/permissions/android.ts` (`AndroidPermissions`, implements `PermissionsPort`) over React Native's `PermissionsAndroid` (no new dependency, no manifest change, no CAMERA). Foreground and background location, ACTIVITY_RECOGNITION and POST_NOTIFICATIONS; API levels below 29/33 report the newer ones as granted. Android cannot say "blocked" directly, so a permission that is not granted, was asked before (flag kept in the plain store) and no longer allows a rationale is `blocked`; `never_ask_again` is `blocked` at once. `openAppSettings` uses `Linking.openSettings`; battery guidance stays with `Tracker.requestBatteryOptimizationSettings` (the ladder's battery step). `defaultServices` now uses it instead of `notWiredPermissions` (which stays as a test placeholder).
- Tests: 10 in `test/permissions.android.test.ts` with a modelled Android (full ladder walk, names asked, denied then blocked, restart, granted later in settings, old API levels, location switch reader). 109 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- Not done / unverified: the "location switch off" reader is an injected hook that is not wired (the system switch needs a native call; assumed on, so that ladder warning never fires on a phone yet). The asked-flags live in the plain store, which is still memory-only until M4.11c, so after a restart a blocked permission reads as undetermined until then. `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale` on `PermissionsAndroid` is not in React Native's typed API, it is used through a guarded cast and falls back to "blocked"-leaning false; not exercised on a phone.

The active/deadhead split is shown only when `modules.stops` is on AND that shift's `segmenter` is `stops`, always with the "estimated" asterisk; total distance never has one; a missing deduction reads "not available", an empty distance is a dash, never 0. The nav is now Shifts, Devices, Settings; `/` and unknown paths go to the first visible screen (the old placeholder Home is gone).

## PR M3.4 — Upgrade flow (Server track)

- Done: `src/db/upgrade.ts` (`upgradeDatabase`), called from `startServer` before the app serves anything (the database is opened with `openDatabase`, no longer `openAndMigrate`). `pendingMigrations()` in `migrate.ts` reads what is pending without writing, and refuses a database that names a migration this build does not know (`MigrationError`, the process exits; checked first, so no backup is made). With pending migrations and real data (a user exists), a verified backup goes to `pre-migration/` first (engine only: `backup_runs` may not exist at the old schema, so the row is added with the new `service.record()` after the migration succeeded; last 3 kept). If that backup fails, **no migration runs**; if a migration fails, the single transaction rolls back and the old schema is intact. In both cases the server comes up with mode `read_only` (new `ServerState`: `mode`, `reason`, `migration` report with `status`, `pending`, `backup`, `error`): a hook answers every `/api` request except `/system/status` with 503 `read_only_mode` (`Retry-After: 30`, retryable, detail = the reason, which names the backup file to restore); generally, in `read_only` mode for another reason, only writes would be refused. `/healthz` stays 200. A new empty database migrates without any backup. The nightly job is not started in read-only mode, and the instance headers are skipped (the old schema may lack the columns the code selects).
- Tests: 8 new in `upgrade.test.ts`: nothing pending; with data the backup is made and recorded, the migration applies; a failing migration (creates a table, then errors) leaves tables and `schema_migrations` unchanged, mode read-only, backup kept, reason names it, HTTP reads and writes are 503; a failed backup (no key) blocks the migration; the read-only hook; a newer database is refused with no backup; a brand-new database needs no backup; an older-schema database with data (built from migrations without 0006) is upgraded, data kept, backup present. `db.test.ts` (startServer refusing a newer database) still passes through the new start path.
- Deviations / not done: "status during migration" is the `state.migration` report plus the 503 detail; there is no `/system/status` yet (M3.5 adds it and should return `mode` and `migration`), and the server does not listen while the pre-migration backup runs. Roadmap fallback: if B9 shows it is unreachable too long (Docker `HEALTHCHECK` start period is 10 s), start listening first; that is a decision after B9, not built. A legacy instance that has data but no age key (created before M3.2) cannot make the backup and stays blocked until a key is configured; no such deployment exists. Size about 330 lines of source and tests.

## PR M4.11b - Keystore-backed credential store (Phone adapters)

- `apps/phone/src/storage/secureStore.ts`: `SecureKeyValueStore` (implements `KeyValueStore`) over `expo-secure-store` (new dependency `~56.0.4`, adds no manifest permission; Android Keystore AES key, app already has `allowBackup=false`). Keys are mapped to the characters SecureStore accepts. A value the Keystore cannot decrypt is deleted and read as absent, so the phone shows "not paired" and the driver re-pairs; `unreadable` lists such keys. `defaultServices` now uses it for `secure` (the pairing credential), so pairing survives an app restart. The native module is `require`d lazily.
- Tests: 6 in `test/secureStore.test.ts` against a fake Keystore (round trip, key mapping, lost key, credential survives a new `Pairing` over the same store and is the only secure key, unpair).
- Unverified: the Keystore itself on the S24 Ultra (survival across a real kill and reboot; behaviour after a lock-screen change). Nothing ran on a phone. The plain store is still memory-only (next PR).

## PR M4.11c - Durable plain storage (Phone adapters)

- `apps/phone/src/storage/sqliteStore.ts`: `SqliteKeyValueStore` (implements `KeyValueStore`) over `expo-sqlite` (new dependency `~56.0.6`, no manifest permission): one table `kv(k, v)` in the app-private `milewage-kv.db`, WAL, upsert per key, so a write is atomic. Chosen over reusing the native buffer DB (that one is Kotlin-owned and has no generic table; touching it would need Kotlin + JUnit + a Gradle run) and over AsyncStorage (2 MB row read limit on Android, the diagnostics ring is larger). A failed open is retried on the next call. `defaultServices` now uses it as the `plain` store: the active shift, cached settings (`GET /settings`), the diagnostics ring and state, and the permission asked-flags survive a restart. The credential stays in the Keystore store (M4.11b).
- Tests: 7 in `test/storage.test.ts` against real SQLite files (Node's `node:sqlite` behind the `SqlDb` interface, `test/support/nodeSql.ts`): CRUD, values survive close and reopen, a 2.4 MB value, open retry, and three restart cases (the active shift resumes and its live distance re-attaches, the diagnostics ring, a blocked permission). 122 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- Unverified: `expo-sqlite` on a phone (only the interface was tested, against Node's SQLite). The diagnostics ring is still written only at the existing persist points (start, 10-minute window, crash handler), so events recorded since the last persist are lost on a hard kill; that cadence is unchanged. Whether 1.6 MB JSON rewrites every window are acceptable for battery is unmeasured.
