Each needs its own "absent when off" test in the PR that adds the screen (17 section 8 item 5).


## PR M3.5 — Low-disk rules, GET /system/status, log rotation (Server track)

- Done: `src/disk.ts`: `diskStateOf` is the table of `10` section 11.3: below 200 MB free `writes_blocked`; else below twice the database size (db plus WAL) `backup_skipped`; else below 1 GB or below 10% of the volume `warning`; else `ok`. `createDiskMonitor` reads `statfs` of the data directory (cached for 1 s). **507 once, in core**: a hook in `installHooks` answers every non-GET request that carries a credential with 507 `insufficient_storage` (`Retry-After: 60`, retryable) while `writes_blocked`, so it holds with every module on or off; `/auth/login` and `/auth/logout` are exempt, and so is everything that is a GET (reads, exports, backup download). A request without any credential is not told about the disk (it gets its normal 401). The backup engine refuses to start below twice the database size (`BackupError` reason `disk`, nothing is written, older backups are kept; `POST /backups` maps it to 507, the nightly job logs it); this also protects the pre-migration and pre-restore copies. Verbose diagnostics are accepted and discarded whenever the disk state is not `ok` (`discarded` count; the `diag.self` event is not written yet). `GET /system/status` (session only; 401 anonymous, 403 for a device): `app_version`, `schema_version` (number of applied migrations), `sqlite_version`, `db_bytes`, `wal_bytes`, `disk_free_bytes`, `disk_state`, `last_backup`, `offsite` (`reminder_due` only once a backup exists and neither a download in 30 days nor a "synced elsewhere" within 90 days), `paired_device_count` (not revoked), `mode`, and `diagnostics` (`level`, `db_bytes`, `event_count`) when that module is on. It stays reachable while `restoring` and after a blocked upgrade. Log rotation: both compose services use `json-file` with `max-size 10m` and `max-file 3`.
- Tests: 10 new in `status.test.ts` (threshold table with every boundary; 507 on seven different writes including a phone's telemetry and diagnostics, reads/health/status/login/logout still fine, anonymous callers learn nothing, recovery; 507 with tracking, devices and diagnostics off; backup skipped at 700 MB free with a 400 MB database then recovers; verbose diagnostics discarded; status shape/auth/values, last backup and reminder logic over 91 days, revoked device, `restoring` mode, diagnostics block absent with module off; compose logging options). `npm run smoke:docker` passed against the real image: `/system/status` is 401 through Caddy, both containers report `json-file` 10m x 3, and the server starts through the new upgrade path.
- Deviations / not done: the test harness now runs on a pretend volume (100 GB free) so the suite never depends on the host's disk. "Log rotation" for the application itself is Docker's json-file driver only (10 section 11.2); the server writes nothing else. Banners are the web track's job. The `diag.self` accounting of `disk_low` drops, the retention pruning of diagnostics under low disk (capture data) and new-capture refusal belong with the capture module. `SystemStatus` does not carry the migration report or the reason for `read_only`; the 503 `read_only_mode` detail has the reason. If the web client needs them as fields, that is a small `packages/shared` OpenAPI change (not mine to edit inside this PR). Unverified on a real nearly-full disk: B10 on the VM.
## PR M7.15 — Serve the built web client from Caddy (Web track; additive infra change)

- Not a numbered PR in the M7 table; it is the "serve static files via Caddy" step of the web assignment, kept small. `infra/docker/Dockerfile`: a `web` stage (`npm run build:web` on the existing build stage) and a `caddy` target (`caddy:2.8.4-alpine` plus the built client in `/srv/web`), placed BEFORE the server stage so the server stays the last stage and a plain build is unchanged. `docker-compose.yml`: the caddy service now builds that target (image `milewage-caddy:0.1.0`). `Caddyfile`: the web screens `/`, `/shifts`, `/shifts/*`, `/devices`, `/settings` answer with `index.html` (no-cache); `/assets/*` are served immutable; every other unknown path is still a 404 and the API handlers are untouched. The route list is a copy of `apps/web/src/screens.tsx` and must be kept in step by hand (a comment says so). `smoke.sh` gains one check that `/shifts` serves the client.
- Verified for real: a Caddy container with the real Caddyfile and a built `dist` returned 200 html for the five screens, 404 for `/nope`, `/index.html` and a missing asset, immutable cache headers on assets; `docker build --target caddy` succeeded; `npm run smoke:docker` passed twice (before and after the new check).
- Limits: the web build bakes in no `VITE_MAP_STYLE_URL` (blank basemap) unless the image build is given one; a new web screen needs its path added to the Caddyfile by hand.


## PR M4.11d - Tracker read-by-shift, exact live distance after restart (Phone adapters)

- Kotlin: `TrackerBuffer.readByShift(shiftClientId, afterSeq, limit)` (PENDING and UPLOADED rows of one shift, oldest first, read only; quarantined and evicted rows are not in the buffer) with index `idx_telemetry_shift (shift_client_id, seq)`; module function `readByShift`; `readPending` now shares the same private reader. TS: `Tracker.readByShift`, native types, `FakeTracker`, contract test. `ShiftController.load()` now seeds the live accumulator from all stored rows of the active shift (pages of 10,000; the accumulator ignores a seq it has seen, so rows the feed announces again are not double counted), so the figure after a restart equals the uninterrupted one. The screen text "Approximate; the server calculates the total" stays (the live figure is still the engine's running value, not the server's).
- Tests: JUnit 2 new (TrackerBufferTest now 9; whole module 18, run through `:comma-tracker:testReleaseUnitTest` in `scripts/phone-release-build.sh`, passed); jest 3 new (contract, restart equals uninterrupted with some rows UPLOADED, a 10,050-row shift across two pages). 125 pass, 1 skipped in total.
- Build: the Gradle release build ran for real on this machine (APK 83 MB, was 75 MB: expo-sqlite and expo-secure-store native code) and the merged-manifest lint ran against it. It showed that `expo-secure-store` pulls `USE_BIOMETRIC` and `USE_FINGERPRINT` into the merged manifest (not declared by us, not on the old forbidden list). They are now in the plugin's strip list (as are `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, which `expo-file-system`, an Expo dependency, had been putting into the merged manifest since before this PR), and a new jest check asserts the merged release manifest holds only the six declared permissions plus INTERNET and VIBRATE (and the app's own dynamic-receiver permission); verified after a rebuild. No CAMERA.
- Unverified: `readByShift` on a phone (the module glue compiles but was not executed).

## PR M4.11e - shift.end manifest and phone_result (Phone adapters)

- `apps/phone/src/shift/endPayload.ts` (`buildEndExtras`): after the upload barrier, reads every stored row of the shift with `Tracker.readByShift`, runs `processShift` (shift start, the stored end time, the segmenter captured at start) and adds to `POST /shifts/{ref}/end` the fields of 08 section 4.4 / 14 section 9.3: `manifest {first_seq, last_seq, point_count}` and `phone_result {calc_version, segmenter, input_digest, total_m, active_m, deadhead_m, personal_m, trips_count}`, typed from the OpenAPI `PointManifest` / `PhoneResult` schemas. Same inputs give the same body on a retry. Sent only when the `tracking` module is on and the shift has stored points; any engine error sends the plain `{ended_at}` (ending never depends on the engine). M5 is not required.
- NOT sent, on purpose: `interruptions` (the native recovery path that would write them (09 section 5.1 item 6, 14 section 7.3) does not exist yet) and the clock-skew input of 14 section 15. The `recon` in the response is ignored until M5.3. Points the server quarantined are no longer in the buffer, so they are in neither the manifest nor the digest (the server does not hold them either, so both sides use the same set); this means the `points_missing` reason can not be raised by a quarantine on the phone side. Stops stay without labels (labels are web only).
- Tests: 4 in `test/shift.test.tsx` (body equals an independent `processShift` run plus manifest from the stored rows with some uploaded; a retry sends identical extras; no points or tracking off sends only `ended_at`; an engine failure still ends). 131 pass in total.
- Unverified: the Hermes run time of `processShift` at shift end (A12 still open) and whether the server's `input_digest` equals this one on a real shift (the server shares the same engine, the digest depends on the same fixes; not tested end to end because the server is another track).

## PR M4.11f - Vehicle picker and the 409-on-retry prompt (Phone adapters)

- Home screen: with more than one vehicle a radio list picks the vehicle (the previous shift's vehicle is preselected, remembered in the plain store as `shift.lastVehicleId`); with one vehicle it is shown by name; the old "first vehicle" rule is gone.
- 409 `shift_already_active` on a RETRIED create (a shift started offline, then the server turned out to hold another active one) no longer drops the local shift. `ActiveShift.conflict` (persisted) keeps the server's shift; the tracker keeps recording and the points stay buffered. Home shows: "Another shift is already running on the server...", with "Keep this shift and try later" (`keepLocalShift`, the prompt is not raised again for that shift) and "Continue the server shift instead" (`useServerShift`: stops and forgets the local shift, continues the server's; says on screen that this drops the miles recorded in the local shift). A 409 at the first start (online) keeps the existing "Continue that shift" prompt.
- Tests: 7 new (3 controller conflict cases incl. restart persistence, last vehicle, 3 screen cases: picker start with vehicle 4, single vehicle, conflict prompt and continue). 138 pass in total.
- Deviations / open: after "Continue the server shift instead" the local shift's stored points are not uploaded (the server has no such shift: the upload worker parks them after three 404s, 08 section 4.3); they stay in the buffer for the 14 day retention and are not shown anywhere. There is no "end my local shift and keep its miles" path because the server allows one active shift only; whether the owner wants one (for example a local-only archive) is a product question. The picker lists names only (the server's vehicle list shape was used as is).


## PR M3.6 — Spike B tests B1-B17 ported to infra/vm-tests (Server track)

- Done: `infra/vm-tests/` (runner `run.mjs`, `lib/` for the API client, load generator, docker helpers and stats, `tools/seed.cjs`, `docker-compose.vm.yml`, `README.md`). The tests drive the shipped compose stack through the real API (setup, confirm, sign-in, pairing, telemetry, diagnostics, `POST /backups`, `POST /setup/restore`, `GET /system/status`); no test hooks exist in the product; what the API cannot show is read with a read-only SQLite connection inside the container. Tests: b01-b05, b15 (B5b), b06-b14, b16, b17 with the thresholds of `10` section 15 and the M2/M3 exit tables. Differences from the spike rig are listed in the README: B3 needs about 56 paired phones because each phone is limited to 60 batches/min; backups wait for the 15-minute limit; B4's reader uses `GET /shifts/{id}` and the list because the route-history read does not exist yet; B13 uses the modules that exist; B9 forces a failing migration by forgetting that `core/0006` was applied (its column stays, so the ALTER fails) and then drops the column for the real run. Also in this PR: the SQLite version gate of `10` section 3.3 (`sqliteVersionOk`, checked in `openDatabase`, refuses to start below 3.51.3 / 3.50.7 / 3.44.6 or 3.52+), needed by B12.
- Tests (dry run, `npm run verify`): `vm-tests.test.mjs`, 11 tests: all 17 tests are listed with spec references, `list` and unknown-test exit codes, report verdicts, percentile and argument helpers, phones needed per rate, `DRY_RUN` command construction, `docker compose config` of the override next to the shipped file (data volume replaced, limits kept), and against the real server in process: setup/sign-in/re-sign-in with a stale cookie, pairing, the generator storing every point once with journal, series and reader, the B7 island logic including a deliberately deleted row, the B6 resend of only unacknowledged batches (duplicates are idempotent), the diagnostics stream, and a hand-posted generator batch. Plus 2 SQLite-gate tests in `db.test.ts`.
- Local smoke on a 16-core development machine with Docker 29.1.3, short durations, **NOT Spike B results**: b12, b03, b04, b05, b06, b07 (container kill standing in for the power pull), b08 (1.78 M points restored in 9.7 s through `POST /setup/restore`, counts equal), b09 with 300,000 seeded rows (forced failure left old schema and rows intact and the server read-only with a pre-migration backup; the real migration then applied), b11, b13, b14 (with the Go `age` 1.1.1), b15 (single writer: backup done in 18 s; with a second writing connection it did not finish within a 60 s cap, consistent with the `10` section 3.1 rule), b16, b17, b01 and b02 at 1 to 2 minutes. They ran end to end and found real bugs in the scripts which are fixed here. Not exercised: b10 (needs a root loop mount), the real power pull, full-length runs, anything on the 1 vCPU / 1 GB VM. Preview numbers worth a look on the VM: the stack read 125 MB RSS idle and 169 MB peak under B3 on the dev machine against budgets of 100 and 150 MB (Go and Node size themselves to the CPU count, so the VM may differ), and the API alone peaked at 121 to 149 MB in b05/b14 against the 120 MB line. If the VM confirms it, the roadmap says the Human decides whether to rebase the budget.
- Size and deviations: far over the 400-line cap (about 1,900 lines including the README and prettier's expansion of the runner); it is one self-contained directory and was kept as one PR. The server does not report event-loop lag, so the B16 lag comparison is recorded as "not measured". `milewage-spikes/B` was not deleted: the roadmap says to remove it only after M3 exits on the VM.

- Done: `pages/Backups.tsx` (core screen `/backups`): `GET /backups` list with kind, size and the verification result, "Back up now" (`POST /backups`; a 429 shows the server's text and wait), `GET /system/status` warnings (low disk, `backup_skipped`, `writes_blocked`, read-only after a failed upgrade, restore running, off-site reminder due) and a small status block. The Download link is shown only when `modules.backup_download` is on. `pages/Restore.tsx`: reached from the setup screen ("Restore from a backup instead"), multipart `POST /setup/restore` (file + recovery key), shows the server's verification counts, then "Continue to sign in". `/backups` added to the Caddyfile SPA list and to the screen registry.
- Tests: 10 new in `backups.test.tsx` (list, empty, run and read back, 429, warnings, no warning, download absent/present by flag, restore success + multipart, refused key, no restore entry on a ready server); `moduleflags.test.tsx` navigation expectations updated. 78 web tests in total.
- Not done / limits: the recovery key cannot be shown again (the server has no route; the existing "type the last 6" path is the only one). `PUT /backups/offsite-ack` and `GET /backups/{id}/download` are not built in the server, so there is no "synced elsewhere" control and the download link depends on a flag that is false today. The multipart body is not read by the test fake (jsdom file streams hang under Node's Request), so only the content type is asserted. Not run in a browser.

## PR M5.3 — Shift end runs the engine, reconciliation, trips/stops/flags storage (Server track)

- Done: `POST /shifts/{ref}/end` now runs `processShift` from `@milewage/track-engine` on the points the server stores (module `tracking` on), in one transaction with the status change: totals, `calc_version`, `calculated_at`, `trips` (auto rows, replaced on every run), `shift_flags` and, with the `stops` module on, `stops`. New migrations: `core/0007_trips.sql` (`trips`, `shift_flags`), `stops/0001_stops.sql` (`stops`, `stop_labels`). New module `stops` (depends on core, tracking; routes follow in the next PR) so `stopsOn()` is the registry, and a new shift gets `segmenter = stops` only when the module is on and `stops_enabled` is on; the engine always uses the shift's own `segmenter`, never the setting. The body now validates `manifest`, `phone_result` (digest `^[0-9a-f]{16}$`) and `interruptions` as in OpenAPI and stores `phone_total_m`, `phone_calc_version`, `input_digest`, `interruptions_json`. The response carries `recon {status, server_total_m, diff_m, reasons}` when the phone sent a result; a retry rebuilds the same `recon` from the stored columns. `Shift.flags` is now filled (list, read and end).
- Reconciliation (`processing/recon.ts`, pure): equal digest and equal numbers = match; equal digest and equal calc_version but different numbers = `engine_disagreement` + `calc_mismatch` column + flag `calc_mismatch` (review); otherwise the reasons in the order of 15 section 7.3 (`points_missing` when the stored points with a seq in the manifest range are fewer than `point_count`, `calc_version`, `edited_on_web`, `late_points`, else `unknown`).
- Tests: 21 new in `end.test.ts` (stored totals/trips/stops equal an independent engine run, retry, match, points_missing, calc_version, engine_disagreement, unknown, phone_missing, no points, interruptions, validation, segmenter fixed at start, stops module off, tracking off, reconcile table). 150 server tests in total; `npm run verify` passes. Three existing tests needed `stops: false` where they switch `tracking` off (stops depends on tracking), one expects `total_m: 0` instead of null for an ended shift without points.
- Deviations / interpretations: (1) different digest but equal `total_m` is `match`, as 15 section 7.2 says (14 section 9.4 does not cover this cell); the split (active/deadhead) is not compared in that case. (2) No `phone_result` stores `recon_status = phone_missing` and returns no `recon` member (OpenAPI: present only with a phone result). (3) `edited_on_web` and `late_points` are implemented in `reconcile` but only fed by later recomputes (labels, late points: next PRs); the first `shift.end` cannot raise them. (4) Flag severity `review` for `gap_large`, `label_orphaned`, `override_orphaned`, `calc_mismatch`, `clock_skew` (the spec has no severity table). (5) Trip overrides, manual trips, `finalize`, `recalculate` and the late-point recompute (M5.9, M6.4) are NOT built; the engine is run without edits. (6) `deduction_cents` is still null (country packs, M9.2).


## PR M6.3 — Stops module routes, trips list, route read (Server track; the route read is also M7.6s)

- Done: `GET /shifts/{ref}/stops` (web session only), `PUT` and `DELETE /shifts/{ref}/stop-labels/{stop_key}` (module `stops`), `GET /shifts/{ref}/trips` (core; session or device inside the 14 day window), `GET /shifts/{ref}/route` (new module `replay`, depends on tracking; raw stored points as parallel arrays `t, lat_e7, lon_e7, speed_dmps`, `source: raw`). All three stop routes answer 404 `not_found` while `settings.stops_enabled` is off; the `stops` and `stop_labels` rows are kept and the past shift is unchanged (checked). A label (`PUT`, last writer wins on `edited_at`, a tie or older edit returns the current row with `X-MileWage-Applied: false`) or its removal re-runs the shared engine on the shift (`processor.run`, the shift's own `segmenter`) and returns `{stop, shift}`; the split moves, `total_m` does not (checked against an independent engine run). A label whose stop disappears after a recompute stays stored and raises `label_orphaned` from the engine.
- Contract change (OpenAPI + regenerated types): `StopLabelResult.stop` is nullable, null only when DELETE cleared a label whose stop no longer exists, so an orphan can be cleared at all.
- Tests: 14 new in `stops.test.ts` (list shape and auth, label recompute vs independent engine, idempotence and LWW, delete, orphan clear, orphan flag, refusals incl. active/finalized/422/403, `stops_enabled` off then on, trips for web and phone, route arrays, 14 day window, replay off, stops module off). 164 server tests in total. `test/support.ts` gets `TRACKING_OFF` (tracking and everything that depends on it) so tests that turn tracking off do not break each time a dependent module is added.
- Deviations: `PUT` on a `stop_key` that is not a current stop of the shift is 404 (the result needs a stop); labels become orphans only through a recompute. Labelling an `active` shift is 409 `shift_not_ended` (the engine runs at the end); a finalized shift is 409 `shift_finalized`. Not done in this PR: `recalculate`, `finalize`, late-point recompute (M6.4), the `live` event `stops` (with the live PR), `change_log` entries for label edits (sync PR), the `overwrite_log` for a replaced label (sync PR).


## PR M7.9s — GET /summary and the odometer routes (Server track)

- Done: `GET /summary?from=&to=` (web session only): `shift_count`, `hours`, `active_m`, `deadhead_m`, `personal_m`, `total_m` over ended and finalized shifts whose `local_date` is in the period (both ends inclusive; active shifts are not counted); `earnings_cents`, `tips_cents` and `hourly_earnings_cents` come only from the driver's stop labels (a label with no payout or tip counts as "not entered"), and are null while the stops setting or module is off. Without `from`/`to` the period is the current month in the owner's time zone. `GET /odometer` (vehicle, kind, local date window in the owner's zone, newest first, at most 500), `POST /odometer` (year_start / year_end, vehicle must exist, not more than 5 minutes ahead) and `DELETE /odometer/{id}` (any reading; a reading of a finalized shift is 409 `shift_finalized`). `localDate` moved to `src/time.ts`. Core reads label sums through a `labelsOn()` port so it does not import the stops module.
- Tests: 8 new in `summary.test.ts` (empty period, inclusive window and exclusions, default month, earnings from labels and hiding when the stops setting is off, validation, access; odometer create/list/filter/delete, shift readings and finalized guard, validation, access). 172 server tests in total. No OpenAPI change was needed (the documented shapes already match).
- Deviations: `deduction_cents` is always null until the country packs exist (M9.2 slot `deduction` is in place in `summaryRoute`). Two year readings for the same vehicle and year are allowed (the spec has no uniqueness rule); the driver deletes the wrong one. `hours` is rounded to 0.001.


## PR M7.7s — GET /live WebSocket (Server track; committed as "M7.14s" by mistake, the roadmap id is M7.7s)

- Done: module `live` (depends on core). `GET /api/v1/live` upgrades to a WebSocket for a web session cookie whose `Origin` equals the `Host` (401 without a session, 403 for a wrong origin or a device bearer, written on the upgrade socket; a plain GET answers 426 `upgrade_required`, a new catalog code). Frames as in 08 section 6 and the `Ws*` schemas: `hello` (server time, active shift or null, plus `device_online` map), `position` (newest point of an accepted batch, at most one per 2 s per shift), `shift` (`started`, `ended`, `updated`; `finalized` is wired but nothing emits it yet), `stops` (after a shift end or a label; only with the stops module and setting on), `device` (online/offline from `last_seen_at` against 3 x 60 s, checked on telemetry and on every ping sweep), `ping` every 30 s with the connection closed after 2 missed pongs. Other modules announce facts on the event bus (`shift.started|ended|updated`, `telemetry.accepted`, `stops.derived`); `ServerRouteHost` now carries `bus`, and the live hub subscribes, so shifts, telemetry and stops do not import it. New dependency: `ws` ^8.22.1 (already in the tree through Expo, now direct) and `@types/ws`; the esbuild bundle still builds.
- Tests: 9 new in `live.test.ts` over a real listening server and the `ws` client (refusals, hello, shift lifecycle with throttled positions, device online, ended with stops, label pushes stops again, offline sweep, stops setting off, mid-shift hello, ping/pong close, module off). 182 server tests in total. The settings test that used a fake `live` module as "an unregistered module" now uses `capture`.
- Deviations: with the module off an upgrade request gets no HTTP answer (Node closes the socket when no `upgrade` listener exists) rather than a 404; the plain GET is 404 as for any disabled route. The upgrade bypasses the Fastify hooks (rate limit, setup confirmation gate): the session check is the same code (`Authenticator`). Client frames other than `pong` are ignored. `clock_skew_ms` on `device` frames is always null (the server does not keep a per-device skew yet).


## PR M5.6 — sync module: change_log, overwrite_log, GET /sync/changes (Server track)

- Done: module `sync` (core, devices). Migration `sync/0001_change_log.sql` (`change_log`, `overwrite_log`). The module listens on the event bus, so core, tracking and stops do not know it: `shift.*` -> a `shift` upsert plus an upsert of every current trip and a `delete` for trip keys it logged earlier that are gone (tombstones); `odometer.updated`, `vehicle.updated`, `settings.updated` -> entries; `edit.overwritten` -> `overwrite_log` (a shift PATCH that changes notes or vehicle, a shift odometer PUT that changes the reading, a replaced stop label). New events in `packages/shared/src/events.ts` (`odometer.updated`, `vehicle.updated`, `settings.updated`, `edit.overwritten`); odometer, vehicle and settings routes now emit. `GET /sync/changes?after=&limit=` (session or phone): log rows carry no data, the response gives the CURRENT entity in its API shape (an entity that no longer exists is reported as `delete`), an older entry for a key already in the page is dropped, a phone sees only shifts in its 14 day window or touched by a device in that window (and their trips/odometer), vehicles and settings always. `410 cursor_expired` (new catalog code) when entries after the cursor were pruned, or the cursor is ahead of the log (restore from an older backup). A daily job prunes entries older than 30 days. With the module off the route is 404 and neither table is written.
- Tests: 9 new in `sync.test.ts` (shape and order, paging by 2 gives the same final states, web edits/vehicle/settings, tombstones for a deleted odometer reading and for trips a label recompute removed, device window and "device wrote to it", 410 after pruning and for a future cursor, overwrite_log, validation/access, module off writes nothing). 193 server tests in total; the bundle still builds. Tests that turn `devices` off now also turn `sync` off.
- Deviations: `override` entries and `orphaned_override` are not produced (trip overrides are not built, M5.9). Trips are re-logged on every `shift.updated` (noise, harmless: the phone upserts by key). `shift.finalized` is subscribed but nothing emits it yet (finalize route is M6.4). Stop labels are not an entity of their own: their effect reaches the phone as changed trips and shift. The prune job is a 24 h interval started with the server, not wall-clock scheduled.


## PR M3.7 — Backup download, off-site acknowledgement, shift-end trigger (Server track)

- Done: `GET /backups/{id}/download` (web session; the `.db.gz.age` file streamed as `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment`; a row that failed verification or whose file is missing is 404; the download resets the 30 day off-site reminder at the moment the file is handed out) and `PUT /backups/offsite-ack {synced_elsewhere}` (sets or clears `offsite_synced_elsewhere_at`, returns `OffsiteState`; the reminder logic moved into `offsiteState()` in `status.ts` and `GET /system/status` uses the same function). Both routes belong to `backup.download` (new switch, default on: `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD=0|1` or `backup.download` in `config/settings.json`) and answer 404 when it is off; `GET /settings` reports it as `modules.backup_download`. The backup module now subscribes to `shift.ended` behind `backup.on_shift_end` (default on, `MILEWAGE_BACKUP_ON_SHIFT_END` or settings file): it runs a `shift_end` backup in the background, skipped when a manual or shift-end backup ran in the last 15 minutes (10 section 13), failures are logged and never reach the shift request.
- Tests: 10 new in `backup-offsite.test.ts` (download bytes equal the stored file and its sha256 and are age ciphertext, reminder resets and returns after 31 days, 404/401/403/422 cases, unverified row refused, ack for 90 days then asks again, validation, no reminder before a first backup, flag in settings, download off, shift-end backup and its 15 minute limit, shift-end off, config switches). 203 server tests in total. The test harness turns the shift-end backup off by default (a real backup after every test shift end would race the teardown); tests opt in with `backupOnShiftEnd: true`.
- Notes: retention keeps the newest backup of a day, so a second `shift_end` backup on the same day replaces the first row (existing behaviour of `selectExpired`, observed in the test). Not verified: a download of a multi-hundred-megabyte file through Caddy (only the stream path was tested in process).


## PR M9.2 — Country packs and the deduction value (Server track)

- Done: `packages/shared/src/countries.ts`: `CountryPack` (unit, `periodStart`, `deduction({totalM, priorM, date, vehicleKind})` in cents or null, `sources`, `verified`, `notes`), a registry (`createCountryRegistry`, process-wide `countryPacks`; a new country registers without touching anything else) and packs for CA, US, GB. Rates: CA provincial 70/64 (2024), 72/66 (2025), 73/67 (2026) cents per km, 5,000 km tier per calendar year; US 67 (2024), 70 (2025), 72.5 (1 Jan 2026), 76 (1 Jul 2026) cents per mile, by the shift's local date; GB cars/vans 55p to 10,000 miles then 25p from 6 April 2026 (45p before), motorcycles 24p, tax year from 6 April, bicycles no rate. Dates with no registered rate, bicycles (CA, US, GB) and countries without a pack give `null`, never a guess. `Shift.deduction_cents` and `GET /summary.deduction_cents` are now computed at read time (`processing/presenter.ts`: per shift, with the business metres already driven in the same rate period and vehicle kind, so a tier splits correctly inside and across shifts; the period figure sums the shifts' own values); nothing is stored. Every route that returns a shift (list, read, end, stops, live, sync) goes through `presentShift`.
- Figures were read on 2026-11-05 from canada.ca, irs.gov and gov.uk through a fetch tool that summarises the page; they are the primary pages but NOT compared by a person, so every pack has `verified: false` (the roadmap keeps HMRC fields and rates "unverified" until a human checks; the GB note says so). For GB only the 2026-27 page was read; 45p for earlier years rests on its "before 6 April 2026" sentence. CA territory rates are not used. `settings.country` validation still uses `DEFAULT_COUNTRIES` (US, CA, GB), unchanged.
- Tests: 5 in `packages/shared/test/countries.test.ts` (dated rates, tiers, tax year start, null cases, stub registration without edits) and 4 in `summary.test.ts` (two dates in one period, no rate or active shift, Canada tier shared across shifts and a later period, no pack). 240 tests in `apps/server` + `packages/shared`.
- Not done in M9.2: the PDF layouts, `logElements`, and the CRA/IRS/HMRC log-element claims (need the Human, M9.4).


## PR M9.1+M9.3 — Exporter registry, CSV (trip and shift level), GPX, GeoJSON (Server track)

- Done: module `exporters` and `GET /exports/{format}` (web session only; `from`+`to` at most 366 days apart as local dates, or `shift_id`; optional `vehicle_id`; `level=trip|shift` for CSV). `src/exporters/registry.ts` is the attach point (`createExporterRegistry`, `Exporter {format, contentType, extension, requires, run}`); a format that is unregistered, switched off (`modules.export_<format>` in the settings file or `MILEWAGE_MODULE_EXPORT_<FORMAT>=0`) or whose required modules are off (gpx and geojson need `tracking`) is a plain 404 and is absent from `GET /settings.modules.exporters`. The body is streamed from a generator (one shift's points at a time, not a live cursor, because the single connection is shared). CSV: trip level has the 12 columns of 08 section 11 (local date and times in the owner's zone, `lat lon` with 6 decimals, distance in the owner's unit with 2 decimals, `kind (estimated)` for auto trips of a `stops` shift, `platform` of the labelled stop that opened an active segment, `source` auto/manual); shift level has the columns of 04 section 2.3 with Miles or Km in the names and `Deduction_Value` instead of `IRS_Deduction_Value`, plus a `Split_Estimated` column; platforms, deliveries, gross pay and tips are sums over web labels and blank when nothing was labelled (and blank when the stops setting is off); RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, formula guard (`'` prefix on text starting with = + - @ tab CR). GPX 1.1 (one `trk` per shift, raw stored fixes, ele/time/speed) and GeoJSON (LineString per shift with totals, Point per stop with role and confidence, `[lon, lat]`): no payouts, labels, places or notes (checked by string search).
- Also here: a rate is no longer carried into a year nobody announced (US rates stop at 2026-12-31, GB at the 2026-27 tax year end, both registered only from 2024); `exportFormatsOn` config helper.
- Tests: 15 in `exports.test.ts` (columns, CRLF, units, estimated marker, platform from label, blanks vs money, setting off, cell quoting and guard, selection, validation incl. 365/366 days, access, GPX counts/time/no secrets, GeoJSON structure/no secrets, stops off, format list with pdf absent, tracking off, exporters off, a stub exporter registered without editing anything else). 256 tests in `apps/server` + `packages/shared`.
- NOT done / deviations: PDF (M9.4: no PDF library is installed and the roadmap says to register it only when its dependency exists; `pdf` is therefore a 404 although the OpenAPI enum lists it), archived-route "simplified" wording (there is no route archive yet, M6.6), odometer cross-checks in exports (M9.5), overlap warning in CSV. The previous push contains a commit titled "fixup" (test typing for M9.2) because the amend was refused by the sandbox; no force-push was used. Streaming of a year of data through Caddy and the 1 GB RAM bound are unmeasured.


## PR M8.1s — Diagnostics retention job, size cap and diag.self (Server track, partial M8.1)

- Done: `DiagnosticsStore.pruneExpired` (rows past `expires_at`: basic 90 days, verbose 14, crash 365; transactions of at most 5,000 rows; then `incremental_vacuum`; new files are created with `auto_vacuum = incremental`, an existing file keeps its mode) and `enforceCap` (events cap 100 MB; measured as payload size plus a 120 byte allowance per row, not file size, because deleting scattered rows leaves half-empty pages: verbose rows oldest first, then the oldest rows other than `crash`, only as many as the excess needs). `diagnostics/keeper.ts`: a daily job (`diagnostics-housekeeping`, module `diagnostics`) that prunes, enforces the cap and writes one server `diag.self` event (`src: server`, `dropped {ring_full, schema_reject, level_off, disk_low}`, `pending 0`, `ring_kb 0`, `cpu_ms_per_h` = whole-process CPU over the interval, i.e. an upper bound, `writes_per_h`), then resets its counters. Ingest counts what it refuses (`schema_reject`) or discards (`level_off`, `disk_low`). While the disk state is `writes_blocked` the pass does nothing and the counts are kept and reported on recovery (16 section 7.2). The catalogue validator accepts the report.
- Tests: 7 new in `diagnostics-keeper.test.ts` (retention per level incl. crash, batching, cap order and crash protection, `diag.self` content and reset, paused on `writes_blocked`, valid catalogue event, no report without an owner, and the real job inside a server with a 30 ms interval).
- NOT done (rest of M8.1 and M8): the full phone catalogue ingest beyond what M2.10/M2.11 built, `GET /diagnostics/events|health|bundle` (health score computations), rollups (`diag_daily`, 2 years), the capture module (M8.5), the redacted bundle and canary suite (M8.4), the B17 idle-cost measurement, and the server-side `ring_full` count (the server has no ring). The nightly job is an interval from server start, not a 03:00 wall-clock slot.

A new screen whose path is missing in the Caddyfile (or the reverse) now fails `npm run verify` instead of 404ing in production.

## PR M7.5b — Trips table in shift detail (Web track)

- Done: `pages/Trips.tsx` (`TripsTable`, `kindLabel`) reads `GET /shifts/{ref}/trips` (core, always present) and shows start, end, distance in the driver's unit, kind, purpose and source in the shared sortable table (keyboard, CSV copy). Auto trips of a stops-segmented shift read `active (estimated)` / `deadhead (estimated)`; manual and personal trips carry no marker. With the stops module/setting off, or a shift not segmented by stops, active and deadhead read `business`, so the split never leaks. A failed read says so in place and does not break the page. Trips are read again when the shift's `calculated_at` changes (a stop label recompute, next PR). Test fixtures moved to `test/support/fixtures.ts`.

## PR M7.8 — Stops table, labelling and estimate warnings (Web track)

- Done: `pages/Stops.tsx` (`StopsPanel`), shown in shift detail only when `modules.stops` is on (the effective flag: module and setting). It reads `GET /shifts/{ref}/stops` and lists time, dwell, confidence, the engine's estimate (header `Estimate*`, footnote) and, per row, controls for role (pickup/dropoff/other), platform, place, payout and tip in whole currency units (converted to cents; an amount that is not money is refused locally). Save = `PUT /shifts/{ref}/stop-labels/{stop_key}` (`edited_at` = now, existing notes kept), Clear = `DELETE`. The response's recomputed shift replaces the detail's shift, so the estimated split above updates and the trips table reads again. Warnings: stacked orders (a pickup after a pickup with no drop-off between, from the role actually used), edited trips (shift flag `manual_trip_present`: labelling may unlink those edits), and "label not applied" (shift flag `label_orphaned`). A server refusal (e.g. 422 on the platform pattern) shows in the row. Table uses the shared sortable table (keyboard, CSV copy).
- Tests: 12 new in `stops.test.tsx` (columns and marker, save with cents and recompute shown, clear, bad amount makes no call, 422 text, three warnings plus the no-warning case, empty, helpers, stops module off = no section and no request). 97 web tests in total.
- Deviations / limits: no engine "edited trip" flag exists; `manual_trip_present` is the closest signal (trip overrides are not built). The stacked-orders warning is a heuristic from the role sequence, not an engine flag. Notes on a label have no input (kept as stored). A second PUT for the same stop from another tab is last-writer-wins on `edited_at` (server); the UI does not show `X-MileWage-Applied`. Stop markers on the map are not drawn (the route view has no stops layer). Not run in a browser.

Hourly earnings (`$20.00 / h`; `not entered` when null with stops on, never zero; `unavailable` with stops off), deduction value (`not available` plus a note when the country has no pack), and the active/deadhead split with the estimate marker and footnote. With stops off the third metric becomes "Business distance" (active + deadhead) with no marker. Periods: last 30 days (default), this month, this year, as local dates in the instance time zone. One summary line (shifts, hours, total distance). Path added to `infra/docker/Caddyfile` (the M7.15b test guards it).

## PR M5.9 (1 of 2) — Trip overrides, manual trips, PATCH/DELETE /trips (Server track)

- Done: migration `tracking/0002_trip_overrides.sql` (`trip_overrides`, PK shift + trip_key). `PATCH /trips/{id}` (web or phone): an auto trip gets/updates a `trip_overrides` row (kind and purpose; the missing field is taken from the current trip; the edit time is the server clock), a manual trip is updated in place; `DELETE /trips/{id}` (web only) removes a manual trip only (`409 trip_not_editable` for auto trips); `PUT /shifts/{ref}/trips/{client_id}` (idempotent manual trip, `trip_key` = the lower-cased client id, last-writer-wins on `edited_at`, tie to the server, `X-MileWage-Applied`); `PUT /shifts/{ref}/trip-overrides/{trip_key}` (module `tracking`, same LWW rule; a key with no trip is kept and answered with `trip: null` plus the `override_orphaned` flag). Every edit recomputes the shift: the engine reads the overrides as `edits` (`originalKind`, times and distance are never touched), manual trips are added to the totals and raise the `manual_trip_present` flag, `trips.edited_at` carries the override time. With `tracking` off only manual trips exist and `processor.retotal` sums the stored trips (no engine run). A replaced override or manual trip goes to `overwrite_log` (`edit.overwritten`, entities `trip_override` and `trip`). Sync feed: `override` entries (upsert, or `orphaned_override` when no trip has the key, re-sent after every recompute), manual trip upserts, and a `delete` tombstone for a deleted manual trip (new optional fields `tripKey`, `deleted` on the `trip.updated` event). A device sees manual trips and overrides only inside its 14 day window.
- Contract change (OpenAPI + regenerated types): `TripEditResult.trip` is nullable (null only for an orphaned override), the same kind of change as `StopLabelResult.stop`.
- Tests: 15 new in `trips.test.ts` (override via PATCH vs an independent engine run, survives a recompute, LWW incl. tie/older/newer and the overwrite log, orphan flag and `orphaned_override`, feed upserts, refusals, manual trip totals/flag/idempotence/replace/PATCH/DELETE, feed delete, tracking off). 534 tests in the workspace run by vitest; `npm run verify` passes.
- Deviations: `PATCH /trips/{id}` on an auto trip while `tracking` is off is `409 trip_not_editable` (no auto trips exist then, except old ones). Edits on an `active` shift are stored and applied when the shift ends (no recompute). Manual trips are not in the phone's `recon` comparison (recon compares the two engines only). A manual trip's time window is not forced inside the shift. The shift purge (`DELETE /shifts/{ref}`) is the next PR.


## PR M5.9 (2 of 2) — DELETE /shifts/{ref} purge (Server track)

- Done: `DELETE /shifts/{ref}` (web only). An `active` shift is `409 shift_not_ended`; a shift that started less than 7 years ago needs `?confirm_recent=true` (otherwise `422 validation_failed` with path `/confirm_recent`, 07 section 4.2); then the shift row is deleted and the `ON DELETE CASCADE` children go with it (points, trips, stops, labels, overrides, flags, readings; `overlaps_shift_id` of another shift is cleared first). New event `shift.deleted` (ids and keys collected before the delete); the sync module writes `delete` tombstones for the shift, its trips, overrides and odometer readings. A purged shift's tombstones are visible to a phone even though the shift row no longer exists (the 14 day window cannot be checked on a missing row).
- Contract change (OpenAPI, types regenerated): `purgeShift` also lists `422`, and its summary states the two refusals.
- Tests: 4 new in `purge.test.ts` (confirmation rule, cascade over every table, 8 year old shift without confirmation, another shift untouched, active/device/anonymous refusals, tombstones in the feed). `npm run verify` passes.
- Not done: nothing is written to the backup or the live feed for a purge (the live feed would only matter for an open web page); a purge does not remove diagnostics events that mention the shift (they carry no shift ids).


## PR M7.10 — Odometer logbook (Web track)

- Done: core screen `/odometer` (after Shifts; path in the Caddyfile). Vehicle choice (only when there are several), a form to add a fiscal-year start or end reading (`POST /odometer`; typed value in the driver's unit converted to metres, date turned into the start of that local day in the instance zone, daylight saving and far-east zones tested; future dates and non-numbers refused before any call), and two tables from `GET /odometer` per kind: fiscal-year readings (with Delete) and shift readings (read-only, linked to the shift). For Canada a note gives the CRA requirement (readings at the start and end of each fiscal year), the base-year rule (a 12-month logbook as base, then a 3-month sample while use stays within 10%) and says MileWage has no base-year or sample mode; it adds "no year-start reading for YYYY yet" when this year has none. Other countries get a one-line "readings are optional".
- Tests: 10 new in `odometer.test.tsx`. 114 web tests in total; `moduleflags.test.tsx` navigation expectations updated.
- Limits: shift readings are not editable here (`PUT /shifts/{ref}/odometer/{kind}`; next PR puts that in shift detail). The CRA wording rests on 07 section 4.1 and is not re-checked against canada.ca by a person. "This year" and "year" are calendar years (a fiscal year that is not the calendar year is not modelled). Two year-start readings in a year are allowed by the server; the page lists both. No GPS cross-check (M9.5). Not run in a browser.


## PR M7.10b — Shift odometer readings in shift detail (Web track)

- Done: `pages/ShiftOdometer.tsx`, a core section of shift detail with the shift's start and end reading in the driver's unit. It reads `GET /odometer` (the shift's vehicle and local day, per kind, keeping only readings whose `shift_id` is this shift) and saves with `PUT /shifts/{ref}/odometer/{kind}` (`recorded_at` = now). A non-number is refused locally; a server refusal (e.g. 409 on a finalized shift) shows in place; a finalized shift is shown read-only. Completes the "shift readings" half of M7.10.
- Tests: 4 new in `shiftodometer.test.tsx` (query by vehicle and day and only this shift's rows, save in metres, local and server refusals, finalized read-only). 118 web tests in total.
- Limits: the reading is matched by the shift's `local_date`; if the server stored it under a different local date (reading recorded past midnight) it is not found and shows blank. Not run in a browser.
## PR M6.4 — Finalize, recalculate, late-point recompute with debounce (Server track)

- Done: `POST /shifts/{ref}/finalize` (web only; `ended` -> `finalized`, idempotent, `active` is `409 shift_not_ended`; emits `shift.finalized`, which the live feed and the sync log already subscribed to). After it every write is `409 shift_finalized` (points, labels, trip edits and overrides, notes, odometer, recalculate; checked in one test that compares the whole shift before and after). `POST /shifts/{ref}/recalculate` (web only): the shared engine on the stored points with the shift's own segmenter, overrides re-applied, manual trips and labels kept, `calc_version` follows the engine; `active` is `409 shift_not_ended`, finalized is `409 shift_finalized`; with `tracking` off it adds up the stored trips instead. Late points: `processing/late-recompute.ts`, owned by the `tracking` module (`telemetry.accepted` subscription and a job that clears pending timers on stop). A batch accepted for an `ended` shift starts or restarts a per-shift 5 s timer (`lateRecomputeMs` in tests; a burst can postpone it at most 30 s); when it fires it re-reads the shift and does nothing unless it is still `ended` (so a shift finalized in the meantime is never recomputed), then runs the engine, and emits `shift.updated` (the sync log and the live feed follow) and `stops.derived`.
- Recon after a later recompute (`reconFromStored`, pure): compares the engine total with the phone total stored at shift end (the split is not stored, so only the total is compared), with the reasons of 15 section 7.3 (`calc_version`, `edited_on_web`, `late_points`, else `unknown`; equal digest and version with a different total stays `engine_disagreement` and sets `calc_mismatch`). Every recompute (labels, trip edits, recalculate, late points) now refreshes `recon_*`.
- Tests: 10 new in `finalize.test.ts` (finalize rules, nothing changes on a finalized shift, finalize between a late batch and its timer, recalculate restoring a stale row, overrides and manual trips kept, tracking-off recalculate, a debounced burst gives exactly one run, recon `late_points`, match staying match, engine disagreement surviving a recompute, active shift not computed). `npm run verify` passes.
- Deviations / not done: no "reopen a finalized shift" route (14 section 11 says a driver reopens deliberately; OpenAPI has no operation for it, so the decision on its shape is left to the Human). `edited_on_web` is "a web-made override, a label or a manual trip exists", not "changed after the phone's run" (no per-edit audit of that). The recalculate of a shift whose raw points were archived is added with the archive job (M6.6). The debounce timer lives in memory: a restart in the 5 s window loses it, the next recalculate or late batch covers it.


## PR M7.10c — Shift odometer lookup spans a shift past midnight (Web track)

- Fix to M7.10b: the shift detail's odometer lookup now runs from the shift's `local_date` to the local day it ended on, so an end reading entered after midnight is found (the M7.10b "Limits" line about this no longer applies). 1 new test; 119 web tests in total.


## PR M3.7w — Off-site acknowledgement control and download reminder (Web track)

- Done: on the Backups page, an "Off-site copy" section shown only when `modules.backup_download` is on: the last download time, and a checkbox "My backup folder is synced to another place" that sends `PUT /backups/offsite-ack {synced_elsewhere}` and shows the server's returned state ("Confirmed ..., you will be asked again after 90 days"; a confirmation older than 90 days reads as not ticked with a request to tick again). Clicking Download hides the "no copy has left this server" reminder and shows "Last download: just now" at once (the server resets the reminder when it hands the file out), without a reload. The existing Download link was already gated by the same flag. With the flag off there is no section, no checkbox and no ack call.
- Tests: 7 new in `offsite.test.tsx` (last download, tick, untick, stale, download click, refusal, module off). 126 web tests in total.
- Limits: the real file download through Caddy was not tried; the reminder reset on screen assumes the browser actually completes the request. Not run in a browser.


## PR M9.1w — Export buttons (Web track)

- Done: `pages/Exports.tsx`. Screen `/exports` (path in the Caddyfile), visible only when `modules.exporters` is not empty: from/to dates (default last 30 days, in the instance zone), a vehicle choice when there are several, "CSV rows" (trip or shift level) when csv is registered, and one "Download X" button per registered format only (so pdf has no button while the server has no PDF exporter). The period is checked before any call (both dates, start not after end, at most 366 days). The shift detail gets "Export this shift" buttons (`shift_id`) under the same flag. The file is fetched (`parseAs: blob`) so a refusal such as the 429 rate limit or a 422 is shown in place, then saved under the server's `Content-Disposition` name (fallback `milewage-<period>.<ext>`). The page says that GPX and GeoJSON carry no payouts, labels or notes and that no file is claimed to meet a tax office's rules.
- Tests: 12 new in `exports.test.tsx` (buttons per format, query for period/vehicle/level, no level for GPX, bad periods make no call, 429 text and nothing saved, per-shift button asks `shift_id=1`, helpers, exporters off = no screen/section/request and URL falls to the first visible screen, a single registered format). `kit.tsx` now passes response headers on success too. 137 web tests in total; `moduleflags.test.tsx` navigation updated.
- Limits: the file is held in browser memory as a Blob before saving (a year of CSV is fine; a very large GPX is not measured). A refused download is not retried. The real streamed download through Caddy and the browser save dialog were not tried. Not run in a browser.
## PR M6.6 — Route archive and raw-point retention (Server track)

- Done: migration `tracking/0003_route_archives.sql` (07 section 2.6). Job `route-archive` (module `tracking`, daily, first run after at most 60 s; `archiveEveryMs` in tests; at most 25 shifts per run): a **finalized** shift whose `calc_version` equals the engine's and whose end is older than `settings.raw_retention_days` (default 90, minimum 7), or an **unfinalized** shift older than the longer of that window and 180 days (it gets the `archived_unfinalized` info flag as the web notice), has its points replaced by `route_archives`: Douglas-Peucker at 5 m (`simplifyRoute` of the engine, 50 dm) run separately between kept points, keeping the first and last point and the first and last fix of every detected stop; GeoJSON `LineString` with `properties.times` (epoch seconds). The archive row is inserted, read back (vertex count) and the points deleted (count must equal `raw_point_count`) in one transaction; a failed check rolls everything back. Totals, trips, stops and labels are untouched. `GET /shifts/{ref}/route` answers `source: archive` (speeds null) after the archive; GPX and GeoJSON are written from the archive, GPX says `[simplified]` in the track name and the file description, GeoJSON sets `simplified: true` (this closes the M9.3 "archived shifts say simplified" item).
- Because an archived shift has no points, the engine must not run on it: `processor.run` throws the new `409 not_recomputable` (added to the error catalogue and to the 409 row of 08 section 7.1); recalculate, stop labels and override/auto-trip edits are refused before anything is written; manual-trip edits still work (totals are re-added from the stored trips).
- Tests: 8 new in `archive.test.ts` (simplification unit tests incl. kept stop fixes, archive after the window with totals and trips unchanged, route read and both exports, not recomputable and manual trips still counting, not-due cases incl. old calc_version and 91 day unfinalized, the retention setting, rollback on a failed read-back). `npm run verify` passes.
- Deviations / limits: a finalized shift on an older `calc_version` is never archived (07 section 4.2 rule b, and it cannot be recalculated because it is finalized): it keeps its raw points until the Human decides how a driver reopens a finalized shift (no such route exists, see M6.4 notes). `archived_unfinalized` has no UI yet. The archive time uses the shift's `ended_at`, not the last point. Compression of a year of data and the real size of `geometry` are not measured.


## PR M6.7 — milewage-cli recalc --dry-run (Server track)

- Done: the first `milewage-cli` command (10 section 7, 14 section 11). `apps/server/src/cli.ts` (`runCli`, testable with injected IO) and `cli-main.ts`; `npm run build:server` now also writes `dist/cli.mjs`, and the Dockerfile copies it and installs a `/usr/local/bin/milewage-cli` wrapper, so `docker compose exec api milewage-cli recalc --dry-run [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--json]` works. It opens `milewage.db` **read-only** (no second writing connection, 10 section 3.1), and `processing/recalc-report.ts` runs the engine of this build on every ended or finalized shift (from `--since`, a local date) and prints stored against recomputed totals, the calc versions and the differences; archived shifts are listed as skipped; the last line says "Nothing was changed". Without `--dry-run` it refuses (exit 2): the spec allows only the report for finalized shifts. `shift-processor.ts` exports `buildShiftInput` and `addTrip` so the CLI and the server use the same input and the same manual-trip addition.
- Tests: 6 in `cli.test.ts` (no difference, a stale finalized shift is reported and all tables are byte-identical afterwards, JSON output, manual trips counted, `--since` in both spellings, archived skipped, usage errors, read-only handle) plus the build test now checks `cli.mjs`. `npm run verify` passes.
- Not verified: the Docker image with the wrapper was not built or run in this PR (no `docker compose exec` run). Labels are read for shifts that use the stops segmenter whatever the module toggles say (the CLI has no module config). `restore`, `compact` and other commands named in 10 are not built.


## PR M7.7 — Live map (Web track)

- Done: screen `/live` ("Live map", second in the navigation; path in the Caddyfile), visible only when `modules.live` is on. `live/connection.ts` opens `ws(s)://<page origin>/api/v1/live` (the cookie goes with it; the server requires Origin = Host), answers every `ping` with `{"type":"pong"}`, skips frames that are not JSON or have no `type`, and reconnects after 1, 2, 4 ... 30 s (the wait resets after a successful open); closing the screen closes the socket and stops retrying. `live/state.ts` is a pure reducer over the frames: `hello` (resets the view; keeps the trail only for the same shift), `position` (added to the trail in time order, duplicates dropped, capped at 5,000 points), `shift` started/ended/updated, `stops` (a count, shown only with the stops module on, marked estimated), `device`. `map/LiveMap.tsx` creates the MapLibre map once and only updates the line source and the marker as points arrive (plain background by default, same fallback as the replay map), with a "Follow the newest position" switch. `pages/Live.tsx` shows connection state (Connecting / Live / Connection lost, reconnecting), the running shift (start, elapsed as of the latest update, distance, phone online/offline), last position time and speed, "No shift is running" / "The shift has ended" with a link to Shifts. When the socket connects with a shift already running, its stored route is read once (`GET /shifts/{id}/route`, only with `replay` on) to fill in the trail.
- Tests: 18 new in `live.test.tsx` over a fake WebSocket and a recorded map: ws URL, pong, marker/line/one-map-only, follow on/off, backfill and replay off, no shift / ended, device online/offline, stops count with stops on and off, junk frames, reconnect timing 1 s then 2 s then back to 1 s, unmount closes and stops, module off (no nav entry, no socket, URL falls back), reducer cases, merge/cap, backoff and URL. 154 web tests in total; `moduleflags.test.tsx` navigation updated.
- Deviations / unverified: size is about 800 lines with tests (cap 400); state/connection/map/page were not split because the first two would have been unused code on their own. The generated `Ws*` schemas have `type` typed as the schema name (the generator ignores `const`), so the frame tags are restated in `live/state.ts`; `hello.device_online` (sent by the server, `routes/live.ts`) is not in the OpenAPI schema, handled defensively. A refused upgrade (401/403) cannot be told apart from a network error in the browser; the screen just keeps showing "reconnecting". No real WebSocket or MapLibre rendering ran (jsdom has no WebGL; the socket is a fake): not tested against the real server or through Caddy's WebSocket proxying. The map is blank until the first position; it does not show finished-shift routes or stop markers.

The response is the `DiagEvent` shape of the OpenAPI (`data` parsed). `DiagnosticsStore.list` is the only new store method.

## PR M8.1 (3 of n) — Full catalogue, richer bus payloads, the server's own engine/recon/backup events (Server track)

- Done: `packages/shared/src/diagnostics/kinds.ts` now holds every kind of 16 section 3.2 (added: `activity.transition`, `perm.state`, `upload.drain`, `upload.failure`, `sync.state`, `sync.pull`, `settings.applied`, `storage.state`, `stops.stats`, `stop.detected`, `server.start`, `server.sample`, `api.window`, `recon.result`, `backup.run`, `migration.run`, `restore.run`, `disk.state`, `capture.state`), with a new `objs` field type (array of typed objects) and a `stopKey` pattern whose epoch part is not treated as a coordinate. Event bus (`packages/shared/src/events.ts`): new `engine.ran`, `shift.reconciled`, `telemetry.batch`, `backup.finished` payloads (and earlier `shift.deleted`, richer `trip.updated`). The shift processor announces `engine.ran` (reason shift_end/late_points/label/recalc, ms, stats, flag codes, totals) and `shift.reconciled`; the backup service reports every run (with the longest event-loop stall) through `onFinished`. New `diagnostics/recorder.ts` (the server's `DiagnosticsPort`): validates with the shared schema, honours the level (`effectiveLevel`, shared with ingest), pauses on `writes_blocked`, counts drops for `diag.self`; the diagnostics module subscribes, so no other module imports it. With the module off nothing is recorded.

## PR M7.17 — MapLibre worker in the production build, and a real-browser check of the web client (Web track)

- Found by rendering the built client in a real browser: MapLibre 6 loads its web worker from `assets/maplibre-gl-worker.mjs` (looked up next to the library chunk at run time), which Vite never emitted, so every map (route replay M7.6 and live M7.7) logged "Worker failed to load" and drew no line. Fix: a small Vite plugin in `apps/web/vite.config.ts` emits the worker file under that name; `postcss.config.js` names the Tailwind config by path so a build started from the repository root works. `test/build.test.ts` runs a real production build and asserts the worker file is in `assets/` (2 s). 155 web tests in total.
- Real render (headless Chromium from the Playwright cache, SwiftShader WebGL, driven with `playwright-core` borrowed from another project's node_modules; script and screenshots are in the session scratchpad, nothing added to the repo): the built client served by a stand-in static+proxy server (NOT Caddy: it mimics the same path list, `/assets` and the `/api` and WebSocket proxy) against the real server bundle (`dist/server.mjs`) on a fresh data directory, with a seeded owner (Canada, km), a paired phone, a vehicle and a finished shift with two stops sent through the real telemetry route. Observed working: sign-in; the navigation with every module on; dashboard (deduction $6.94, split 3.2 / 6.3 km marked, earnings "not entered"); shift detail with 3 trips marked estimated and 3 stops; labelling a stop through the UI (saved, Clear appeared, recompute applied); route map with the green line and marker after the worker fix; odometer year-start reading added and listed; CSV downloaded through the browser (549 bytes, columns as 08 section 11, auto trips marked `(estimated)`, platform from the label); manual backup then the Download link and the off-site section present; live map: WebSocket reached "Live", shift start frame shown, positions moved the marker and extended the line, "The shift has ended" after the end. After the fix no console errors remain except the expected 401 before sign-in.
- Not verified: Caddy itself (its `@spa` list is only tested against `screens.tsx`; a real Caddy run was not repeated for the new paths; the stand-in does not prove Caddy's MIME type for `.mjs`, which Go's table maps to text/javascript), mobile widths, light/dark themes beyond the default, the PMTiles/style-URL basemap path, GPX/GeoJSON downloads (only CSV clicked), the stop-label warnings with real data (stacked orders), the backup download file through a browser.
## PR M8.1 (4 of n) — Health, server samples, rollups (Server track)

- Done: `GET /diagnostics/health` (web session only; computed on read): per paired device H1 to H7 with weights 25/20/10/15/10/10/10, green full, amber half, red none, `na` leaves the denominator, score 0 to 100, over the device's last 7 shifts (`diagnostics/health.ts`, thresholds are constants in `HEALTH`); server checks S1 to S5 over the last 24 h (RSS 100/150 MB, event-loop p95 50/100 ms and max under 1,000 ms, last verified backup under 26 h, disk state, 5xx share 0.5/2 %). The server now records `server.start` (once the owner exists; `unclean_shutdown` from a `diagnostics.db.running` marker file), `server.sample` and one `api.window` per route group every 15 minutes (`diagnostics/metrics.ts`: `perf_hooks` event-loop histogram, `process.memoryUsage/cpuUsage`, file sizes, an `onResponse` hook counting statuses, durations and problem codes, ingest counters from the new `telemetry.batch` event). `diag_daily` (migration `0002` in diagnostics.db): `store.rollUp` aggregates complete UTC days before the raw events expire (metrics engine_ms, recon_diff_m, rss_mb_max, lag_p95_ms_max, api_5xx, service_restarts, upload_fail_pct, fixes_per_h, battery_pct_per_h), is idempotent, and drops rows older than 2 years; the daily keeper runs it before the prune.
- Tests: 7 in `diagnostics-health.test.ts` (check thresholds incl. the 7-shift window, server colours, the route and its auth, the server's own samples and windows, rollups). `npm run verify` passes.
- Deviations: the catalogue has no moving-time quantity, so H1 uses the time in windows whose longest fix interval exceeds 60 s plus the gated time (an approximation, labelled); H4 uses `oldest_pending_s` of the newest window as "drained"; H6 reads the server's `recon.result` events of the device's own shift ids; `gap_s_moving` is not rolled up; `migration.run`, `restore.run`, `disk.state` have no producer yet; `server.sample.cpu_pct` is whole-process CPU over the interval. Thresholds are (new) and unvalidated.


## PR M8.4 — Redacted debug bundle and canary suite (Server track)

- Done: `GET /diagnostics/bundle` (web session only; module `diagnostics`; switch `diagnostics.bundle`, default on: `MILEWAGE_DIAGNOSTICS_BUNDLE=0|1` or `diagnostics.bundle` in `config/settings.json`, 404 when off). Query `from`/`to` (local dates of shifts), `shift`. A gzipped JSON document: versions (app, engine, schema count, SQLite, Node), module flags, the device make/model/Android/SDK from its latest `app.start`, the health result with device names replaced by `D1`.. and without `generated_at`, per shift `S1`.. with relative start, duration, totals, segmenter, calc version, recon status and flag **codes** (no detail), and the events re-validated against the shared schema with relative times (seconds from the first event), pseudonymous device and shift ids, `seq`/`received_at`/`cap` dropped, and `stop_key` removed. An event that fails validation or whose text still looks like a coordinate or credential is left out and counted in `excluded_events`. `include_capture` is refused with 422 until the capture module exists.
- Canary suite (`canary.test.ts`, 4 tests): a real run (vehicle `CANARY_BIKE`, shift note `CANARY_NOTE`, device name `CANARY_PHONE`, label place `123 CANARY ST`, payout `987654`, coordinates 43.7653 / -79.3832 as text and as E7 numbers) drives start, telemetry, end with a phone result, label, recalculate and phone diagnostics events; then every table of `diagnostics.db`, the events view, the health view and the bundle bytes are searched: zero hits, and the bundle has no epoch millisecond, no date and no client id. Ingest refuses canaries in version, shift, enum, pattern and undeclared fields. A stored event corrupted after the fact is excluded from the bundle. The suite caught two leaks while being written: `generated_at` and device names in the bundle's health block (both fixed). Phone-side and export-side canaries: the shared-schema canary tests already cover the producer check; the phone is not touched here; GPX/GeoJSON contain coordinates by design and no labels or notes (M9.1 tests).
- Also fixed: `server.start` recorded all modules as off because the registry did not exist yet at job start; it is now built when the event is written.
- Tests: 4 new (11 with health); `npm run verify` passes.


## PR M8.5 — capture module (Server track)

- Done: module `capture` (depends on diagnostics, tracking, devices; **off by default**: `defaultEnabled: false`, `MILEWAGE_MODULE_CAPTURE=1` or `modules.capture` in the settings file). Migration `diagnostics/0003_capture.sql` in diagnostics.db (`capture_sessions`, `capture_fixes`, `capture_annotations`, no foreign keys to the main file). Routes: `POST/GET /diagnostics/capture-sessions` (web; device must be paired and not revoked; hours 1 to 168 default 48; max shifts 1 to 5 default 3; one active session per device: `409 capture_active` (new catalogue code, also in 08 section 7.1); `507` when the disk state is not `ok` or capture data is at the 200 MB cap), `PATCH` (end now), `DELETE` (session, fixes and annotations), `POST .../fixes` (the device's own active session only; up to 500 fixes; idempotent on `(capture, seq)`; a further shift beyond `max_shifts` and an ended or expired session are `409 capture_active`; a shift that received fixes gets the info flag `capture_session`), `PUT/DELETE .../annotations/{client_id}`, and `GET .../replay` (NDJSON: `meta`, `fix`, `annotation`, `shift` lines). Replay is **anonymized by default**: metres east and north of the first fix re-embedded at latitude 0 / longitude 0, times as offsets from the first fix, shift ids `S1..`, `stop_key` rewritten to `S1:<offset>:stop`, annotation text and altitude left out; `mode=exact` returns real values. `GET /settings` gives a device its active session in `diagnostics.capture`. A job deletes sessions 30 days after they ended or expired. Lifecycle facts (`created`, `ended`, `purged`) are recorded as `capture.state` events.
- Tests: 9 in `capture.test.ts` (lifecycle and settings, expiry/low disk/cap, fixes and limits, annotations, anonymized vs exact replay with no place/clock/text in the anonymized bytes, nothing of capture in the bundle, the 30 day purge, off by default, and the U9 check that anonymization keeps a 15 km+ drive's length within 0.1%). `npm run verify` passes.
- Not done / deviations: `include_capture` on the bundle is still refused (422); the `expired` and `started` `capture.state` events have no producer (state is computed on read); the `cap` envelope field of a diagnostics event is still a client-id pattern in the shared validator although the API calls it an integer; the dense stream, the 10 s / 20 m cadence emulation and the phone banner are phone work (not touched); `bytes` of a session is an estimate (48 bytes per fix).


## PR M8 exit — B17 idle cost of the diagnostics module (Server track)

- Done: `scripts/measure-b17.mjs` starts the built bundle (`npm run build:server`) with `MILEWAGE_MODULE_DIAGNOSTICS=0` and `=1`, alternating, `--max-old-space-size=96` as in the image, waits 3 s, then reads `/proc` RSS and CPU ticks over an idle window; median of N rounds; exit 0 only if RSS difference <= 5 MB and CPU difference < 0.1 percentage points (the roadmap thresholds, both marked "new").
- **Local result, NOT a VM result** (16-core development machine, Node 22.22.0, 3 rounds of 20 s idle): RSS 88.15 MB off, 88.31 MB on (+0.16 MB); CPU 0.20 % off, 0.20 % on (difference 0). The first run, with the event-loop-delay histogram at 10 ms resolution, showed +0.2 percentage points CPU (that histogram wakes a timer 100 times a second, always, even at idle); the resolution is now 100 ms, which still records any stall as the delay beyond the interval and costs nothing measurable. Idle only: the 15 minute `server.sample` and the daily housekeeping did not fire inside the window. Not run: the 1 vCPU / 1 GB VM, B16 (ingest at 50 events/s with the module on), the "no timer registered" audit on the server.


## PR M9.5 — Odometer cross-checks in exports (Server track)

- Done: the shift-level CSV gets four trailing columns (existing column positions unchanged): `Odometer_Start_<Miles|Km>`, `Odometer_End_<Miles|Km>`, `Odometer_Check` and `Year_Odometer_Check` (`exporters/odometer.ts`, pure checks plus a read-only reader). Shift check: end minus start against the shift's GPS `total_m`; `ok`, `mismatch: odometer X km; GPS Y km` when they differ by more than 10 % of the larger and more than 1 km, `invalid: ...` when the end reading is below the start, blank when a reading or the total is missing. Year check (the CRA elements, `year_start` and `year_end` readings of the shift's vehicle in the shift's calendar year in the owner's zone): the distance tracked for that vehicle in that year may not exceed the odometer distance by more than 1 % (business plus personal use make the odometer larger, so only "tracked more than driven" is an inconsistency); blank without both readings. A mismatch is reported and nothing is corrected (a test checks the stored total is unchanged and the CSV total is still the GPS figure).
- Tests: 5 in `exports-odometer.test.ts` (pure cases, CSV with no readings, ok then mismatch with the stored total untouched, year ok/mismatch); the shift-level header expectation in `exports.test.ts` has the four new columns. `npm run verify` passes.
- Deviations: the tolerances (10 % / 1 km for a shift, 1 % for a year) are mine, not from a spec; the year check is calendar-year only (a CRA fiscal period that is not the calendar year is not handled); the PDF this was meant to extend does not exist (see M9.4 below), so the checks are in the CSV only; text rows are English only.

README links both. `infra/install.test.mjs`: 4 tests (defaults and mode 600 and no secret, no overwrite, busy port, bad input). The Dockerfile change of M6.7 (the `milewage-cli` wrapper) is now built and run in the image.
- **Verified here (Docker 29.1.3, development machine, not a VM):** `bash infra/docker/smoke.sh` exit 0 with `MILEWAGE_PROXY_SUBNET=172.29.99.0/24 MILEWAGE_HOST_PORT=18081` (the default subnet 172.29.88.0/24 was already taken by another stack on this host: "Pool overlaps", which is exactly what the variable is for); `infra/install.sh --port 18090 --subnet 172.29.97.0/24` brought the stack up healthy end to end (then removed with `down -v`); `docker run --entrypoint milewage-cli milewage-server:0.1.0 recalc --dry-run` runs in the image and says "No database at /app/data/milewage.db".
- **NOT verified / open:** following the guide on a fresh VM or a Pi (M10.1 exit), ARM images, a real tailnet and `tailscale serve`, the source address Caddy sees from it (Spike D), the upgrade-and-rollback drill on the VM (b09 exists and ran locally in M3.6). **Gap found while writing the upgrade doc:** `milewage-cli restore` (10 section 7) is not built, only `POST /setup/restore` on a fresh instance, so the rollback in `upgrade.md` is "start the old version on an empty data volume and restore the pre-migration file there"; the single-command rollback needs that CLI command.


## M9.4 PDF exporter — NOT built, decision (Server track)

- The PDF exporter was skipped on purpose. A dependency small enough to defend does not remove the other two blockers of the roadmap: (1) M9.4 allows no compliance wording until the Human has checked the CRA / IRS / HMRC log elements (claims 1 to 3 of `11` section 4), and a PDF layout is mostly that wording; (2) the M9 exit needs the PDF's RAM for a year of data measured on the 1 GB VM (Spike F), which cannot be done here. The least bad candidate when it is taken up is `pdf-lib` (pure JavaScript, no native code, three small dependencies) built in monthly chunks as the fallback in M9 prescribes; `pdfkit` pulls in font and image parsers and is larger. Nothing is installed. `pdf` stays an unregistered format (404), the registry attach point is ready (`exporters/registry.ts`), and the odometer checks of M9.5 are in the CSV so they can be copied into the PDF's summary page then.

## PR M5.1 — Phone app.db schema and migrations (Phone track)

- Done: `apps/phone/src/db/schema.ts` holds the `app.db` DDL of 15 section 2.2 as migration 1 (`sync_state`, `settings_cache`, `vehicles`, `shifts`, `stops`, `trips`, `odometer_readings`, `outbox`, `conflict_log`, with the CHECKs, indexes and `ON DELETE CASCADE` from the spec). `src/db/appDb.ts`: `AppSqlDb` (the `expo-sqlite` slice plus `getAllAsync` and `withTransactionAsync`), `migrate` (forward only, each migration and its `PRAGMA user_version` bump in one transaction; a failure leaves the old version and rows; a database newer than the app is refused with `MigrationError('newer_than_app')`; a `beforeMigrate` hook runs only when unsynced rows exist and a migration is pending, and a failing hook stops the migration), `openAppDb` (WAL, foreign keys, migrate) and `openExpoAppDb` (file `milewage-app.db`, `expo-sqlite` loaded on first use).
- Tests: 8 new in `appdb.test.ts` over Node's SQLite (tables, idempotent second open, CHECKs and cascade, upgrade with unsynced rows incl. the pre-migration hook, no hook when nothing is unsynced, rollback of a failing migration, hook failure, newer-than-app refusal, file reopened after a "restart"). Test helper `openNodeAppDb`.
- Deviations / not done: `diag_events` and `diag_state` are left out (diagnostics still use the key-value store). The pre-migration file copy of `app.db` and `tracker.db` is only a hook: nothing copies files yet (no file-system module is installed; the real copy arrives with the wiring PR). Nothing reads or writes these tables yet (M5.2 onwards). Not run on a device: `expo-sqlite`'s `withTransactionAsync` is assumed to behave like the Node stand-in.


## PR M5.2 — Phone outbox engine (Phone track)

- Done: `apps/phone/src/sync/outbox.ts` (`Outbox`: durable FIFO in `app.db`; `enqueue` with in-place coalescing of a pending `odometer.put`, `trip.override` and `trip.manual` for the same entity that is not being sent right now, never `shift.create`/`shift.end`/`shift.patch`; `pending`, `parked`, `counts`, `has`, `done` (row deleted), `failed`, `park`, `retryParked`, `dropShift`) and `src/sync/runner.ts` (`OutboxRunner`, one request in flight, oldest first). Entity keys start with the shift `client_id`; each kind maps to its 08 route (`requestFor`). Failure rules: network, 5xx, 429, 507 stop the run, keep the row pending and return the 2/4/8/16/32/60 s backoff (or `Retry-After`); 401/403 halt everything (`repair` for `device_revoked`, else `auth_problem`) until `resume`; 409 `shift_finalized` on anything but a create deletes the row and reports outcome `finalized`; any other 4xx parks the item and holds back only the later items of the same shift. Hooks: `canSend` (the `shift.end` barrier plugs in here, M5.5), `onSent` (carries the response and `X-MileWage-Applied`), `onParked`.
- Tests: 14 new in `outbox.test.ts` (order, exact bodies, coalescing rules, an in-flight item is not rewritten, route mapping, applied header, backoff/Retry-After, parking per shift and `retryParked`, finalized, revoked halt and resume, barrier, per-shift run, single flight and not-paired, kill between answer and delete resends the identical request, restart keeps pending rows).
- Idempotency: there is no separate key header; the keys are the ones the server already enforces (`client_id`, the `PUT` paths, `POST /shifts/{ref}/end` retry-safe). Not wired into the app yet (M5.4 does that); the existing `ShiftController` still sends create/end itself. `diag.batch`/`capture.batch` rows are ignored by this engine on purpose.
